, we obtain a translation vector
{\bm{b}}_{2}=(b_{21},\cdots,b_{2m})\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
with bit complexity
\lceil\log(6N)\rceil
such that

\displaystyle\mathrm{dist}(z_{i,j}-b_{2j},\mathbb{Z})\geq\frac{1}{3N},\quad\forall i\in[N],j\in[d],

(13)

i.e., the translated points
\{{\bm{z}}_{i}-{\bm{b}}_{2}\}_{i\in[N]}
are coordinate-wise far from the integer lattice.
Moreover, by additional translation to
\{{\bm{z}}_{i}-{\bm{b}}_{2}\}_{i\in[N]}
(by adding some natural number, coordinate-wise), we can ensure all coordinates are positive while keeping the property
Equation

13
.
Hence, we may assume without loss of generality
{\bm{b}}_{2}
also has the property

\displaystyle{\bm{z}}_{i}-{\bm{b}}_{2}\geq{\bm{0}}\textrm{ for all }i\in[N].

(14)

Let us denote
{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime\prime}=\{({\bm{z}}_{i}^{\prime},y_{i})\}_{i\in[N]}
, where
{\bm{z}}_{i}^{\prime}:={\bm{z}}_{i}-{\bm{b}}_{2}
.
Then
\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime\prime}}=\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}}(\geq\sqrt{m}/2)
.
For
\rho^{\prime\prime\prime}:=\rho^{\prime\prime}=\frac{1}{4N\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}}}
, we have the robustness radius
\mu^{\prime\prime\prime}:=\rho^{\prime\prime\prime}\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime\prime}}=\rho^{\prime\prime}\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}}=\mu^{\prime\prime}=\frac{1}{4N}
.
Define
f_{\mathrm{trans}}
as
f_{\mathrm{trans}}({\bm{z}}):={\bm{z}}-{\bm{b}}_{2}
.
Then,
f_{\mathrm{trans}}
can be implemented via one hidden layer in a neural network, with
O(m^{2})
parameters.

Upon the two layers constructed from stage I and II, it suffices to construct a network that
\rho^{\prime\prime\prime}
-robustly memorizes
{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime\prime}
since the translation preserves separation and ball containment properties.
Note that the robustness balls after stage II are not affected when passing the
{\sigma}
, by
Equations

13
and
14
.

Stage III
(Grid Indexing)
From
Equation

13
, each
{\bm{z}}_{i}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
is at least
\frac{4}{3}\mu^{\prime\prime\prime}
distant away from any lattice hyperplane
H_{z,j}:=\{{\bm{z}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{m}\mid z_{j}=z\}
with any
j\in[m]
and
z\in\mathbb{Z}
.
Thus, each robustness ball of
{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime\prime}
lies completely within a single integer lattice (or unit grid) of the form
\prod_{j=1}^{m}[n_{j},n_{j}+1)
, for some
(n_{1},\cdots,n_{m})\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{m}
.

Moreover, as
\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime\prime}}\geq\sqrt{m}/2
, for any
i\neq i^{\prime}
with
y_{i}\neq y_{i^{\prime}}
, we have
\left\lVert{\bm{z}}_{i}^{\prime}-{\bm{z}}_{i^{\prime}}^{\prime}\right\rVert_{2}\geq\sqrt{m}
.
Since
\sup\{\left\lVert{\bm{z}}-{\bm{z}}^{\prime}\right\rVert_{2}\,\mid\,{\bm{z}},{\bm{z}}^{\prime}\in\prod_{j=1}^{m}[n_{j},n_{j}+1)\}=\sqrt{m}
, two such points
{\bm{z}}_{i}^{\prime}
and
{\bm{z}}_{i^{\prime}}^{\prime}
that corresponds to distinct labels cannot lie in the same grid.
Since each
\mu^{\prime\prime\prime}
-ball lies within a single grid, we conclude that no two
\mu^{\prime\prime\prime}
-ball with different labels lie within the same grid.

We define
R:=\lceil\max_{i\in[N]}\left\lVert{\bm{z}}_{i}^{\prime}\right\rVert_{\infty}(=\max_{i\in[N],j\in[m]}(z_{i,j}^{\prime}))\rceil\in{\mathbb{N}}
.
Our goal in this stage is to construct
\mathrm{Flatten}
mapping defined as

\mathrm{Flatten}({\bm{z}}):=R^{m-1}\lfloor z_{1}\rfloor+R^{m-2}\lfloor z_{2}\rfloor+\cdots+\lfloor z_{m}\rfloor.

This maps each grid
\prod_{j=1}^{m}[n_{j},n_{j+1})
onto the point
\sum_{j=1}^{m}R^{j-1}n_{j}
.

However, since
\mathrm{Flatten}
is discontinuous due to the use of floor functions, we construct
\overline{\mathrm{Flatten}}
, which is a continuous approximation that exactly matches
\mathrm{Flatten}
in the region of our interest.
By applying
Lemma

B.16
to
\gamma=\frac{1}{4N}
and
n=\lceil\log_{2}R\rceil
, we obtain the network
\overline{{\mathrm{Floor}}}:=\overline{{\mathrm{Floor}}_{\lceil\log_{2}R\rceil}}
with
O(\log_{2}R)
parameters such that

\displaystyle\overline{{\mathrm{Floor}}}(z)=\lfloor z\rfloor\quad\forall z\in[0,R]\textrm{ with }z-\lfloor z\rfloor>\frac{1}{4N}.

(15)

Moreover, since we apply
\gamma=1/4N
to
Lemma

B.16
, the lemma guarantees that
\overline{{\mathrm{Floor}}_{n}}
can be exactly implemented with
O(n+\log N)=O(\log R+\log N)
bit complexity. In particular, we can define our network
\overline{\mathrm{Flatten}}
with
O(\log R+\log N+\log R^{m-1})=O(\log R+\log N+\log N\log R)=\tilde{O}(1)
bit complexity as

\displaystyle\overline{\mathrm{Flatten}}({\bm{z}})=R^{m-1}\overline{{\mathrm{Floor}}}(z_{1})+\cdots+\overline{{\mathrm{Floor}}}(z_{m}).

(16)

This implementation is valid—i.e.
\overline{\mathrm{Flatten}}({\bm{z}})=\mathrm{Flatten}({\bm{z}})
—in the region of interest (
\{{\bm{z}}\in[0,R]^{m}\,|\,\textrm{dist}_{2}(z_{j},{\mathbb{Z}})\geq\frac{1}{2N}\textrm{ for all }j\in[m]\}
) characterized by the margin guaranteed by
Equation

13
.

As
\overline{\mathrm{Floor}}:{\mathbb{R}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}
can be implemented with width 5 and depth
O(\log_{2}R)
network (
Lemma

B.16
),
\overline{\mathrm{Flatten}}
can be implemented with width
5m
and depth
O(\log_{2}R)
network.
Thus, we can construct
\overline{\mathrm{Flatten}}
with
O(m^{2}\log_{2}R)=\tilde{O}(m^{2})
parameters.

By
Equations

13
and
15
, we guarantee that each robustness ball lies in the region where the
\mathrm{Flatten}
is properly approximated by
\overline{\mathrm{Flatten}}
. i.e.

\displaystyle\overline{\mathrm{Flatten}}({\bm{z}})=\mathrm{Flatten}({\bm{z}})\textrm{ for all }{\bm{z}}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{2}({\bm{z}}_{i}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime\prime\prime}).

Since
\mathrm{Flatten}
maps each unit grid into a point and each robustness ball of
{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime\prime}
lies on a single unit grid, we conclude

\displaystyle\overline{\mathrm{Flatten}}({\bm{z}})=\mathrm{Flatten}({\bm{z}})=\mathrm{Flatten}({\bm{z}}_{i}^{\prime})\textrm{ for all }{\bm{z}}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{2}({\bm{z}}_{i}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime\prime\prime}).

Let
m_{i}:=\mathrm{Flatten}({\bm{z}}_{i}^{\prime})
.
Then each robustness ball around
{\bm{x}}_{i}
is mapped to
m_{i}
. We have
m_{i}\in~{\mathbb{Z}}\cap[0,R^{m+1}]
for all
i\in[N]
, since

\displaystyle m_{i}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Flatten}({\bm{z}}_{i}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=R^{m-1}\lfloor z_{i1}^{\prime}\rfloor+R^{m-2}\lfloor z_{i2}^{\prime}\rfloor+\cdots\lfloor z_{im}^{\prime}\rfloor

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}R^{m-1}R+R^{m-2}R+\cdots+R

\displaystyle\leq R^{m+1},

where (a) is by
\left\lVert{\bm{z}}_{i}^{\prime}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq R
.

Stage IV
(Memorization)
Finally, it remains to memorize
N
points
\{(m_{i},y_{i})\}_{i=1}^{N}\subset{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}\times[C]
.
Since multiple robustness balls for
{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime\prime}
with the same label may correspond to the same grid index in Stage III, it is possible that for some
i\neq j
with
y_{i}=y_{j}
, we have
m_{i}=m_{j}
.
Let
N^{\prime}\leq N
denote the number of distinct pairs
(m_{i},y_{i})
.
It remains to memorize these
N^{\prime}
distinct data points in
{\mathbb{R}}
.

Since
m_{i}=\mathrm{Flatten}({\bm{x}}_{i})\leq R^{m+1}
, we apply
Theorem

B.4
from
Vardi et al. 2021
with
r=R^{m+1}
to construct
f_{mem}:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
with width
12
and depth

\tilde{O}(\sqrt{N^{\prime}}\cdot\log(5R^{m}N^{2}\epsilon^{-1}\sqrt{\pi m}))=\tilde{O}(m\sqrt{N^{\prime}})=\tilde{O}(\log N\sqrt{N^{\prime}})=\tilde{O}(\sqrt{N^{\prime}})=\tilde{O}(\sqrt{N})

such that
f_{mem}(m_{i})=y_{i}
.

The final network
f:{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}
is defined as

\displaystyle f({\bm{x}})=f_{\mathrm{mem}}\circ{\sigma}\circ\overline{\mathrm{Flatten}}\circ{\sigma}\circ f_{\mathrm{trans}}\circ{\sigma}\circ(\bar{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}})+{\bm{b}}).

The depth 1 network
\bar{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}})+{\bm{b}}
has width
m
, and also the depth 1 network
f_{\mathrm{trans}}
has width
m
.
\overline{\mathrm{Flatten}}
has width
5m
and depth
O(\log_{2}R)
and
f_{\mathrm{mem}}
has width
12
and depth
\tilde{O}(\sqrt{N})
.
The total construction requires
\tilde{O}(md+m^{2}+m^{2}+\sqrt{N})=\tilde{O}(d+\sqrt{N})
parameters, where each term
md,m^{2},m^{2}
, and
\sqrt{N}
comes from
f_{\mathrm{proj}}
,
f_{\mathrm{trans}}
,
\overline{\mathrm{Flatten}}
, and
f_{\mathrm{mem}}
respectively.
The width of the final network is
\tilde{O}(1)
and the depth is
\tilde{O}(\sqrt{N})
.

The bit complexity of
\bar{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}
is
\tilde{O}(1)
and that of
{\bm{b}}
is

O(\lceil\log(6N)\rceil,\log(\max\{\|\bar{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}})\|_{\infty}\mid{\bm{x}}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{2}({\bm{x}}_{i},\mu)\quad\text{ for some }i\in[N]\}))=\tilde{O}(1).

The network
f_{\mathrm{trans}}
has the bit complexity
\log(\max\{\|{\bm{z}}_{i}\|_{\infty}\mid i\in[N]\})=\tilde{O}(1)
.
\overline{\mathrm{Flatten}}
has the bit complexity
\tilde{O}(1)
, and
f_{\mathrm{mem}}
needs at most
\tilde{O}(\sqrt{N})
.
Hence, the bit complexity of the final network is
\tilde{O}(\sqrt{N})
.
∎

The following is the classical memorization upper bound of parameters used in the proof of
Theorem

B.3

Theorem B.4

(Classical Memorization, Theorem 3.1 from
Vardi et al. 2021
)
.

Let
N,d,C\in\mathbb{N}
, and
r,\epsilon>0
, and let

({\bm{x}}_{1},y_{1}),\dots,({\bm{x}}_{N},y_{N})\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\times[C]

be a set of
N
labeled samples with
\|{\bm{x}}_{i}\|\leq r
for every
i
and
\|{\bm{x}}_{i}-{\bm{x}}_{j}\|\geq 2\epsilon
for every
i\neq j
. Denote
R:=5rN^{2}\epsilon^{-1}\sqrt{\pi d}
. Then, there exists a neural network
F:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
with width
12
and depth

O\left(\sqrt{N\log N}+\sqrt{\frac{N}{\log N}}\cdot\max\left\{\log R,\log C\right\}\right),

and bit complexity bounded by
O(\log d+\sqrt{\frac{N}{\log N}}\cdot\max\{\log R,\log C\})

such that
F({\bm{x}}_{i})=y_{i}
for every
i\in[N]
.

B.2
Sufficient Condition for Near-Perfect Robust Memorization with Moderate Robustness Radius

Theorem B.5
.

Let
\rho\in\Big(0,\frac{1}{5\sqrt{d}}\Big]
, and
\eta\in(0,1)
. For any dataset
{\mathcal{D}}\in{\bm{D}}_{d,N,C}
, there exists
f
with
\tilde{O}(Nd^{\frac{1}{4}}\rho^{\frac{1}{2}})
parameters, depth
\tilde{O}(Nd^{\frac{1}{4}}\rho^{\frac{1}{2}})
, width
\tilde{O}(1)
and bit complexity
\tilde{O}\left(\nicefrac{{1}}{{d^{\frac{1}{4}}\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}}}\right)
that
\rho
-robustly memorizes
{\mathcal{D}}
with error at most
\eta
.

Proof.

For given
\rho
, any desired error
\eta
, and
{\mathcal{D}}=\{({\bm{x}}_{i},y_{i})\}_{i\in[N]}\in{\bm{D}}_{d,N,C}
, we construct a network
f
that
\rho
-robustly memorizes
{\mathcal{D}}
with
\tilde{O}(Nd^{\frac{1}{4}}\rho^{\frac{1}{2}})
parameters.

Stage I
(Projection onto
\log
-scale Dimension and Scaling via the First Hidden Layer Weight Matrix).

The first stage closely follows that of
Theorem

B.3
.
By
Lemma

B.20
, we obtain an integer
m=\tilde{O}(\log N)
and a 1-Lipschitz linear map
\phi:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{m}
such that the projected dataset
{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime}:=\{(\phi({\bm{x}}_{i}),y_{i})\}_{i\in[N]}\in{\bm{D}}_{m,N,C}
satisfies the separation bound

\displaystyle\epsilon_{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime}\geq\frac{5}{12}\sqrt{\frac{m}{d}}\epsilon_{\mathcal{D}}.

(17)

We define
f_{\mathrm{proj}}:{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{m}
as
f_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}})=\frac{11}{9}\cdot\frac{\sqrt{d}}{\epsilon_{\mathcal{D}}}\phi({\bm{x}})
, which is
\frac{11}{9}\cdot\frac{\sqrt{d}}{\epsilon_{\mathcal{D}}}
-Lipschitz.

We apply
Lemma

B.23
with
f_{\mathrm{proj}}
whose depth is
1
,
\nu=\min\left\{\frac{109}{11880}\sqrt{m},\frac{1}{88}\mu,\frac{1}{360N},1\right\}
and
\bar{R}:=\max\{\|{\bm{x}}\|_{2}\mid{\bm{x}}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{2}({\bm{x}}_{i},\mu)\quad\text{ for some }i\in[N]\}
to obtain
\bar{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}
with the same number of parameters, depth and width and
\tilde{O}(1)
bit complexity such that

\displaystyle\max_{\|{\bm{x}}\|_{2}\leq{\bar{R}}}\left\lVert\bar{f}-f\right\rVert_{2}\leq\nu.

(18)

We set the first hidden layer bias
{\bm{b}}\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
so that

\displaystyle\bar{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}})+{\bm{b}}\geq{\bm{0}}\textrm{ for all }i\in[N]\textrm{ and all }{\bm{x}}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{2}({\bm{x}}_{i},\mu),

(19)

where the comparison between two vectors is element-wise.

We obtain the grouping scale
\alpha\in[0,1]
here for Stage II—we call
\alpha
the grouping scale, as we group the points by approximately
N^{\alpha}
points per group in Stage II. From the
\rho
condition, we have
\frac{1}{5\rho\sqrt{d}}\geq 1
. Thus, there exists
\alpha\in[0,1]
such that satisfies
\lceil N^{\alpha}\rceil=\lfloor\frac{1}{5\rho\sqrt{d}}\rfloor
.
Let us bound the
\rho
in terms of
\alpha
. Since
\lceil N^{\alpha}\rceil=\lfloor\frac{1}{5\rho\sqrt{d}}\rfloor\leq\frac{1}{5\rho\sqrt{d}}
, we have

\displaystyle\rho\leq\frac{1}{5\lceil N^{\alpha}\rceil\sqrt{d}}\leq\frac{1}{5\lfloor N^{\alpha}\rfloor\sqrt{d}}.

(20)

We claim that for
{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}:=\{({\sigma}(\bar{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{i})+{\bm{b}}),y_{i})\}_{i\in[N]}\in{\bm{D}}_{m,N,C}
, we have (i)
\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}}\geq\sqrt{m}/2
and (ii) for
\rho^{\prime\prime}:=\frac{1}{4\lfloor N^{\alpha}\rfloor\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}}}
, if
g({\bm{x}})\in{\mathcal{F}}_{m,P}
can
\rho^{\prime\prime}
-robustly memorize
{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}
, then
g\circ{\sigma}\circ(\bar{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{i})+{\bm{b}})
can
\rho
-robustly memorize
{\mathcal{D}}
.
For any
i\neq j
with
y_{i}\neq y_{j}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert{\sigma}(\bar{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{i})+{\bm{b}})-{\sigma}(\bar{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{j})+{\bm{b}})\right\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{=}

\displaystyle\left\lVert(\bar{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{i})+{\bm{b}})-(\bar{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{j})+{\bm{b}})\right\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bar{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{i})-\bar{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{j})\right\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\lVert(\bar{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{i})-{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{i}))-(\bar{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{j})-{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{j}))+({f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{i})-{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{j}))\right\rVert_{2}.

where (a) holds by the construction of
{\bm{b}}
(
Equation

19
).
For simplicity, we denote

\Delta({\bm{x}}_{i},{\bm{x}}_{j}):=(\bar{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{i})-{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{i}))-(\bar{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{j})-{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{j})).

Then, we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert{\sigma}(\bar{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{i})+{\bm{b}})-{\sigma}(\bar{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{j})+{\bm{b}})\right\rVert_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Delta({\bm{x}}_{i},{\bm{x}}_{j})+({f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{i})-{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{j}))\right\rVert_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\geq}

\displaystyle(\|{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{i})-{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{j})\|_{2}-\|\Delta({\bm{x}}_{i},{\bm{x}}_{j})\|_{2})^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{i})-{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{j})\|_{2}^{2}-2\|{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{i})-{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{j})\|_{2}\|\Delta({\bm{x}}_{i},{\bm{x}}_{j})\|_{2}+\|\Delta({\bm{x}}_{i},{\bm{x}}_{j})\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\|{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{i})-{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{j})\|_{2}^{2}-2\|{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{i})-{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{j})\|_{2}\|\Delta({\bm{x}}_{i},{\bm{x}}_{j})\|_{2},

where (a) holds from
\|{\bm{a}}+{\bm{b}}\|^{2}_{2}\geq(\|{\bm{a}}\|_{2}-\|{\bm{b}}\|_{2})^{2}
.
By the construction of
\bar{f}
(
Equation

18
),

\|\Delta({\bm{x}}_{i},{\bm{x}}_{j})\|_{2}\leq\|\bar{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{i})-{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{i})\|_{2}+\|\bar{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{j})-{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{j})\|_{2}\leq 2\nu,

so we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert{\sigma}(\bar{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{i})+{\bm{b}})-{\sigma}(\bar{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{j})+{\bm{b}})\right\rVert_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\|{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{i})-{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{j})\|_{2}^{2}-4\nu\|{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{i})-{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{j})\|_{2}.

(21)

Now we derive

\displaystyle\left\lVert f_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{i})-f_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{j})\right\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{=}\frac{11}{9}\cdot\frac{\sqrt{d}}{\epsilon_{\mathcal{D}}}\left\lVert\phi({\bm{x}}_{i})-\phi({\bm{x}}_{j})\right\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\geq}\frac{11}{9}\cdot\frac{\sqrt{d}}{\epsilon_{\mathcal{D}}}\cdot 2\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\overset{(c)}{\geq}\frac{11}{9}\cdot\frac{\sqrt{d}}{\epsilon_{\mathcal{D}}}\times 2\cdot\frac{5}{12}\sqrt{\frac{m}{d}}\epsilon_{\mathcal{D}}

\displaystyle=\frac{55}{54}\sqrt{m},

(22)

where (a) is by the definition of
f_{\mathrm{proj}}
, (b) is by the definition of
{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime}
and its separation constant, and (c) follows from
Equation

17
.

Plugging this inequality to
Equation

21
gives

\displaystyle\left\lVert{\sigma}(\bar{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{i})+{\bm{b}})-{\sigma}(\bar{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{j})+{\bm{b}})\right\rVert_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\|{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{i})-{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{j})\|_{2}(\frac{55}{54}\sqrt{m}-4\nu)

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\geq}\|{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{i})-{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{j})\|_{2}(\frac{55}{54}\sqrt{m}-\frac{109}{2970}\sqrt{m})

\displaystyle=\|{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{i})-{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{j})\|_{2}\cdot\frac{54}{55}\sqrt{m}

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\geq}\frac{55}{54}\sqrt{m}\cdot\frac{54}{55}\sqrt{m}

\displaystyle=m

where (a) holds from
\nu\leq\frac{109}{11880}\sqrt{m}
, and (b) holds from
Equation

22
.
This proves the first claim
\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}}\geq\sqrt{m}/2
.
To prove the second claim, let
\mu:=\rho\epsilon_{\mathcal{D}}
and
\mu^{\prime\prime}:=\rho^{\prime\prime}\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}}
.
Then,

\displaystyle{\sigma}(\bar{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\mathcal{B}}_{2}({\bm{x}}_{i},\mu))+{\bm{b}})

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{=}\bar{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\mathcal{B}}_{2}({\bm{x}}_{i},\mu))+{\bm{b}}

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\subseteq}f_{\mathrm{proj}}({\mathcal{B}}_{2}({\bm{x}}_{i},\mu+\nu))+{\bm{b}}

\displaystyle\overset{(c)}{\subseteq}f_{\mathrm{proj}}({\mathcal{B}}_{2}({\bm{x}}_{i},\frac{89}{88}\mu))+{\bm{b}}

\displaystyle\overset{(d)}{\subseteq}{\mathcal{B}}_{2}(f_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{i}),\frac{89}{72}\cdot\frac{\sqrt{d}}{\epsilon_{\mathcal{D}}}\times\mu)+{\bm{b}}

\displaystyle\overset{(e)}{=}{\mathcal{B}}_{2}(f_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{i}),\frac{89}{72}\cdot\sqrt{d}\rho)+{\bm{b}}

\displaystyle\overset{(f)}{\subseteq}{\mathcal{B}}_{2}(f_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{i}),\frac{89}{360N})+{\bm{b}}

\displaystyle\overset{(g)}{\subseteq}{\mathcal{B}}_{2}(\bar{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{i}),\frac{89}{360N}+\nu)+{\bm{b}}

\displaystyle\overset{(h)}{\subseteq}{\mathcal{B}}_{2}(\bar{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{i}),\frac{1}{4N})+{\bm{b}}

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{=}{\mathcal{B}}_{2}(\bar{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{i}),\rho^{\prime\prime}\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}})+{\bm{b}}

\displaystyle={\mathcal{B}}_{2}(\bar{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{i})+{\bm{b}},\rho^{\prime\prime}\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}})

\displaystyle\overset{(j)}{=}{\mathcal{B}}_{2}({\sigma}(\bar{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{i})+{\bm{b}}),\rho^{\prime\prime}\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}}),

where (a) and (j) are by
Equation

19
, (b) and (g) are by the construction of
\bar{f}
(
Equation

18
), (c) is because
\nu\leq\frac{1}{88}\mu
, (d) is because
f_{\mathrm{proj}}
is
\frac{11}{9}\cdot\frac{\sqrt{d}}{\epsilon_{\mathcal{D}}}
-Lipschitz, (e) uses
\mu=\rho\epsilon_{\mathcal{D}}
, (f) uses
\rho\leq\frac{1}{5N\sqrt{d}}
, (h) is because
\nu\leq\frac{1}{360N}
and (i) is because
\rho^{\prime\prime}\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}}=\frac{1}{4N\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}}}\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}}=\frac{1}{4N}
.

Hence,
g({\bm{x}})
memorizing the robustness ball
{\mathcal{B}}_{2}({\sigma}(\bar{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{i})+{\bm{b}}),\rho^{\prime\prime}\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}})
on projected space leads to
g\circ{\sigma}\circ(\bar{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}})+{\bm{b}})
memorizing the robustness ball for
{\mathcal{D}}
.
In other words, if
g({\bm{x}})\in{\mathcal{F}}_{m,P}
can
\rho^{\prime\prime}
-robustly memorize
{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}
, then
g\circ{\sigma}\circ(\bar{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}})+{\bm{b}})
can
\rho
-robustly memorize
{\mathcal{D}}
.
With
\rho^{\prime\prime}=\frac{1}{4\lfloor N^{\alpha}\rfloor\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}}}
, Stage II aims to find a
\rho^{\prime\prime}
-robust memorizer
g
of
{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}
. For simplicity of the notation, let
{\bm{z}}_{i}:={\sigma}(\bar{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}}_{i})+{\bm{b}})
for each
i\in[N]
, so that
{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}=\{({\bm{z}}_{i},y_{i})\}_{i\in[N]}
.

Stage II
(Memorizing
N^{\alpha}
Points at Each Layer): Using the grouping scale
\alpha
obtain in Stage I, we group
N
data points to
\lceil N^{1-\alpha}\rceil
groups with index
\{I_{j}\}_{j=1}^{N^{1-\alpha}}
, each with
|I_{j}|\leq\lfloor N^{\alpha}\rfloor+1
. Then, we construct
\tilde{f}_{j}
that memorizes data points and their robustness balls with index
I_{j}
, and the error rate remains small for other data points and their robustness balls.

For each
j\in[\lceil N^{1-\alpha}\rceil]
, we apply
Lemma

B.13
with error rate
\eta\leftarrow\frac{\eta}{N^{1-\alpha}}
,
\alpha\leftarrow\alpha
,
{\mathcal{D}}\leftarrow{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}\in~{\bm{D}}_{m,N,C}
,
\rho\leftarrow\rho^{\prime\prime}
and
I\leftarrow I_{j}
. Then it satisfies that
\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}}\geq\sqrt{m}/2
,
\rho^{\prime\prime}=\frac{1}{4\lfloor N^{\alpha}\rfloor\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}}}
, and
|I|\leq\lfloor N^{\alpha}\rfloor+1
. Thus, we obtain a neural network
\tilde{f}_{j}
with width
O(m)=\tilde{O}(1)
, depth
\tilde{O}(N^{\frac{\alpha}{2}})
and

\tilde{O}\left(N^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}+m^{2}\right)=\tilde{O}\left(N^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}\right)
parameters and bit complexity
\tilde{O}\left(N^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}+m\right)=\tilde{O}\left(N^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}\right)
such that:

\displaystyle\tilde{f}_{j}({\bm{z}})

\displaystyle=y_{i}

\displaystyle\forall

\displaystyle i\in I_{j},{\bm{z}}\in{\mathcal{B}}({\bm{z}}_{i},\rho^{\prime\prime}\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}}),

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{{\bm{z}}\in\operatorname{Unif}({\mathcal{B}}({\bm{z}}_{i},\rho^{\prime\prime}\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}}))}\left[\tilde{f}_{j}({\bm{z}})\in\{0,y_{i}\}\right]

\displaystyle\geq 1-\frac{\eta}{N^{1-\alpha}}\quad

\displaystyle\forall

\displaystyle i\in[N]\backslash I_{j}.

Thus, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{{\bm{z}}\in\operatorname{Unif}({\mathcal{B}}({\bm{z}}_{i},\rho^{\prime\prime}\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}}))}\left[\tilde{f}_{j}({\bm{z}})\in\{0,y_{i}\}\right]\geq 1-\frac{\eta}{N^{1-\alpha}}\quad\forall i\in[N],j\in[N^{1-\alpha}].

(23)

We define for each
j
:

\displaystyle f_{j}\left(\begin{pmatrix}{\bm{z}}\\
y\end{pmatrix}\right)=\begin{pmatrix}{\bm{z}}\\
y+\sigma\left(\tilde{f}_{j}({\bm{z}})-y\right)\end{pmatrix},

so that the last coordinate is given as
y+\sigma\left(\tilde{f}_{j}({\bm{z}})-y\right)=\max\{\tilde{f}_{j}({\bm{z}}),y\}
.
Finally, we define the full robust memorizing network as

\displaystyle f({\bm{x}}):=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{0}\\
1\end{pmatrix}^{\top}f_{\lceil N^{1-\alpha}\rceil}\circ\cdots\circ f_{2}\circ f_{1}\begin{pmatrix}\bar{f}_{\text{proj}}({\bm{x}})+{\bm{b}}\\
0\end{pmatrix}.

We now verify the correctness of the construction. For any
{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{B}({\bm{x}}_{i},\rho\epsilon_{\mathcal{D}})
and
{\bm{z}}=f_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}})\in\mathcal{B}({\bm{z}}_{i},\rho^{\prime\prime}\epsilon_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}})
, since we partition
[N]
into disjoint groups
\{I_{j}\}_{j\in[N^{1-\alpha}]}
, there exists a unique index
j_{i}
such that
i\in I_{j_{i}}
and thus
\tilde{f}_{j_{i}}({\bm{z}})=y_{i}
holds. For all
j\neq j_{i}
, the networks satisfy
\tilde{f}_{j}({\bm{z}})\in\{0,y_{i}\}
with high probability, so none of them can exceed
y_{i}
. Since the final network outputs the maximum among
y
and all
\tilde{f}_{j}({\bm{z}})
, we have
f({\bm{x}})=y_{i}
as long as each
\tilde{f}_{j}({\bm{z}})\in\{0,y_{i}\}
. Therefore, it suffices to show that
\tilde{f}_{j}({\bm{z}})\in\{0,y_{i}\}
holds for
\forall j
, namely,

\left[\tilde{f}_{j}({\bm{z}})\in\{0,y_{i}\}\quad\forall j\in[N^{1-\alpha}]\right]\Rightarrow f({\bm{x}})=y_{i}.

Considering the contrapositive, we have

\displaystyle f({\bm{x}})\neq y_{i}\Rightarrow\left[\tilde{f}_{j}({\bm{z}})\notin\{0,y_{i}\}\quad\textrm{ for some }j\in[N^{1-\alpha}]\right]

Since each
\tilde{f}_{j}
satisfies
\mathbb{P}_{{\bm{z}}\sim\mathrm{Unif}(\mathcal{B}({\bm{z}}_{i},\rho^{\prime\prime}\epsilon_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}}))}[\tilde{f}_{j}({\bm{z}})\in\{0,y_{i}\}]\geq 1-\frac{\eta}{N^{1-\alpha}}
for all
j\in[N^{1-\alpha}]
, we upper bound the error probability using the union bound:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{{\bm{x}}\in\operatorname{Unif}({\mathcal{B}}({\bm{x}}_{i},\rho\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}}))}\left[f({\bm{x}})\neq y_{i}\right]\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{{\bm{z}}\in\operatorname{Unif}({\mathcal{B}}({\bm{z}}_{i},\rho^{\prime\prime}\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}}))}\left[\tilde{f}_{j}({\bm{z}})\notin\{0,y_{i}\}\quad\textrm{ for some }j\in[N^{1-\alpha}]\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in[N^{1-\alpha}]}\mathbb{P}_{{\bm{z}}\in\operatorname{Unif}({\mathcal{B}}({\bm{z}}_{i},\rho^{\prime\prime}\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}}))}\left[\tilde{f}_{j}({\bm{z}})\notin\{0,y_{i}\}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}

\displaystyle{N^{1-\alpha}}\times\left(\frac{\eta}{N^{1-\alpha}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\eta,

where (a) holds by
Equation

23
.
Hence,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{{\bm{x}}\in\operatorname{Unif}({\mathcal{B}}({\bm{x}}_{i},\rho\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}}))}\left[f({\bm{x}})=y_{i}\right]

\displaystyle=1-\mathbb{P}_{{\bm{x}}\in\operatorname{Unif}({\mathcal{B}}({\bm{x}}_{i},\rho\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}}))}\left[f({\bm{x}})\neq y_{i}\right]

\displaystyle\geq 1-\eta.

We verify width, depth and the number of parameters of
f
.
Recall that the final network is

f({\bm{x}}):=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{0}\\
1\end{pmatrix}^{\top}f_{\lceil N^{1-\alpha}\rceil}\circ\cdots\circ f_{2}\circ f_{1}\begin{pmatrix}\bar{f}_{\text{proj}}({\bm{x}})+{\bm{b}}\\
0\end{pmatrix}.

The depth 1 network
\bar{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}})+{\bm{b}}
has width
m=\tilde{O}(1)
. For
j\in[\lceil N^{1-\alpha}\rceil]
, each
\tilde{f}_{j}
has width
\tilde{O}(1)
, depth
\tilde{O}(N^{\frac{\alpha}{2}})
and

\tilde{O}\left(N^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}\right)
parameters.

The network
\bar{f}_{\text{proj}}
needs
dm=\tilde{O}(d)
parameters.
Hence, the number of parameters of
f
is

\tilde{O}\left(N^{1-\alpha}\times N^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}\right)=\tilde{O}\left(N^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}}\right)\overset{(a)}{=}\tilde{O}\left(Nd^{\frac{1}{4}}\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}\right),

where (a) holds by
\lceil N^{\alpha}\rceil=\lfloor\frac{1}{5\rho\sqrt{d}}\rfloor
.
The width of
f
is
\tilde{O}(1)
and the depth of
f
is
\tilde{O}\left(N^{1-\alpha}\times N^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}\right)=\tilde{O}\left(Nd^{\frac{1}{4}}\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)
.

The bit complexity of
\bar{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}
is
\tilde{O}(1)
and that of
{\bm{b}}
is
\log(\max\{\|\bar{f}_{\mathrm{proj}}({\bm{x}})\|_{\infty}\mid{\bm{x}}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{2}({\bm{x}}_{i},\mu)\quad\text{ for some }i\in[N]\})=\tilde{O}(1)
.
The network
f_{j}
has the same bit complexity as
\tilde{f}_{j}
, which is
\tilde{O}\left(N^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}\right)=\tilde{O}\left(\nicefrac{{1}}{{d^{\frac{1}{4}}\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}}}\right)
.
Hence, the bit complexity of the final network is
\tilde{O}\left(\nicefrac{{1}}{{d^{\frac{1}{4}}\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}}}\right)
.

∎

The above construction is motivated by the need to handle overlapped robustness balls with the same label. We transform the construction of classical memorization in
Vardi et al. 2021
in two key directions: first, from memorizing isolated data points
{\bm{x}}_{i}
to memorizing entire robustness neighborhoods
{\mathcal{B}}_{p}({\bm{x}}_{i},\mu)
; and second, to ensuring correct classification even within regions where multiple robustness balls with the same label overlap. To accomplish this, we introduce disjoint, integer-aligned interval encodings and carefully control the error propagation caused by dimension reduction, as addressed in
Lemma

B.11
.

B.2.1
Memorization of Integers with Sublinear Parameters in
N

Lemmas in this subsection are a slight extension of those in
Vardi et al. 2021
, adapted to our integer-based encoding scheme.

From here,
\mathrm{BIN}_{i:j}(n)
denotes the bit string from position
i
to
j
(inclusive) in the binary representation of
n
.
For example,
\mathrm{BIN}_{1:3}(37)=4
, since
(37)_{10}=(100101)_{2}
so that
\mathrm{BIN}_{1:3}(37)=(100)_{2}=(4)_{10}
.

Lemma B.6
.

Let
\eta>0
and
m,n\in\mathbb{N}
with
m<n
. Then, there exists a neural network
F:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
with width 2, depth 2 and bit complexity
\tilde{O}(1)
such that

\displaystyle F(x)=\begin{cases}1&\textrm{ for }x\in[m,n-\eta],\\
0&\textrm{ for }x\in(-\infty,m-\eta]\cup[n,\infty).\end{cases}

Proof.

We construct a network
F
:

F(x)=\sigma\left(1-\sigma\left(-\frac{1}{\eta}(x-m)\right)\right)+\sigma\left(1-\sigma\left(\frac{1}{\eta}(x-(n-\eta))\right)\right)-1.

It satisfies the requirements with depth 2 and width 2. The bit complexity is
O(\log m+\log n+\log(1/\eta))=\tilde{O}(1)
.
∎

Lemma B.7
.

Let
\eta\in(0,1)
, and let
m_{1}<\dots<m_{N}
be natural numbers. Let
N_{1},N_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
satisfy
N_{1}\cdot N_{2}\geq N
, and let
w_{1},\dots,w_{N_{1}}\in\mathbb{N}
. Then, there exists a neural network
F:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
with width
4
, depth
3N_{1}+2
and bit complexity
\tilde{O}(1)
such that,

\displaystyle F(x)=\begin{cases}w_{\lceil\frac{i}{N_{2}}\rceil}&\textrm{ if }x\in[m_{i},m_{i}+1-\eta]\textrm{ for some }i\in[N],\\
0&\textrm{ if }x\notin\bigcup_{j\in[N_{1}]}(m_{(j-1)N_{2}+1}-\eta,\;m_{jN_{2}}+1).\end{cases}

where we define
m_{N+1}=\cdots=m_{N_{1}N_{2}}=m_{N}
.

Proof.

Let
j\in[N_{1}]
. We define network blocks
\tilde{F}_{j}:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
and
F_{j}:\mathbb{R}^{2}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{2}
as follows. By applying
Lemma

B.6
, we construct
\tilde{F}_{j}
such that:

\tilde{F}_{j}(x)=\begin{cases}1&\text{if }x\in\left[m_{(j-1)N_{2}+1},\;m_{jN_{2}}+1-\eta\right],\\
0&\text{if }x\leq m_{(j-1)N_{2}+1}-\eta\text{ or }x\geq m_{jN_{2}}+1.\end{cases}

As a result, for any
i\in[N]
, any
x\in[m_{i},m_{i}+1-\eta]
satisfies

\displaystyle\tilde{F}_{i}(x)=\begin{cases}1&\textrm{ if }i\in[(j-1)\cdot N_{2}+1,j\cdot N_{2}],\\
0&\textrm{ otherwise}.\end{cases}

Next, we define:

F_{j}\left(\begin{pmatrix}x\\
y\end{pmatrix}\right)=\begin{pmatrix}x\\
y+w_{j}\cdot\tilde{F}_{j}(x)\end{pmatrix}~.

Finally, we define the network
F(x)=\begin{pmatrix}0\\
1\end{pmatrix}^{\top}F_{N_{1}}\circ\cdots\circ F_{1}\left(\begin{pmatrix}x\\
0\end{pmatrix}\right).

We now verify the correctness of the construction. For
i\in[N]
, let
x\in[m_{i},m_{i}+1-\eta]
. For
j=\lceil\frac{i}{N_{2}}\rceil
, we have
\tilde{F}_{j}(x)=1
, and for all
j^{\prime}\neq j
,
\tilde{F}_{j^{\prime}}(x)=0
. Therefore, the output of
F
satisfies
F(x)=w_{j}=w_{\lceil\frac{i}{N_{2}}\rceil}
.

The width of each
F_{j}
is at most the width required to implement
\tilde{F}_{j}
, plus two additional units to carry the values of
x
and
y
. Since the width of
\tilde{F}_{j}
is 2, the width of
F
is at most
4
. Each block
F_{j}
has depth 3, and
F
is a composition of
N_{1}
blocks. Additionally, one layer is used for the input to get
x\mapsto\begin{pmatrix}x\\
0\end{pmatrix}
, and another to extract the last coordinate of the final input. Thus, the total depth of
F
is
3N_{1}+2
. The bit complexity is
\tilde{O}(1)
.
∎

Lemma B.8

(Lemma A.7,
Vardi et al. 2021
)
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
and let
i,j\in\mathbb{N}
with
i<j\leq n
. Denote Telgarsky’s triangle function by
\varphi(z):=\sigma(\sigma(2z)-\sigma(4z-2))
. Then, there exists a neural network
F:\mathbb{R}^{2}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{3}
with width
5
and depth
3(j-i+1)
, and bit complexity
n+2
, such that for any
x\in\mathbb{N}
with
x\leq 2^{n}
, if the input of
F
is
\begin{pmatrix}\varphi^{(i-1)}\left(\frac{x}{2^{n}}+\frac{1}{2^{n+1}}\right)\\
\varphi^{(i-1)}\left(\frac{x}{2^{n}}+\frac{1}{2^{n+2}}\right)\end{pmatrix}
, then it outputs:
\begin{pmatrix}\varphi^{(j)}\left(\frac{x}{2^{n}}+\frac{1}{2^{n+1}}\right)\\
\varphi^{(j)}\left(\frac{x}{2^{n}}+\frac{1}{2^{n+2}}\right)\\
\mathrm{BIN}_{i:j}(x)\end{pmatrix}
.

In the following lemma, note that
\rho
does not refer to the robustness ratio.

Lemma B.9

(Extension of Lemma A.5,
Vardi et al. 2021
)
.

Let
\eta>0
, and let
n,\rho,c\in\mathbb{N}
and
u,w\in\mathbb{N}
.
Assume that for all
\ell,k\in\{0,1,\dots,n-1\}
with
\ell\neq k
, the bit segments of
u
satisfy

\mathrm{BIN}_{\rho\cdot\ell+1:\rho\cdot(\ell+1)}(u)\neq\mathrm{BIN}_{\rho\cdot k+1:\rho\cdot(k+1)}(u).

Then, there exists a neural network
F:\mathbb{R}^{3}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
with width
12
, depth
3n\cdot\max\{\rho,c\}+2n+2
and bit complexity
n\max\{\rho,c\}+2
, such that the following holds:

For every
x>0
, if there exist
j\in\{0,1,\dots,n-1\}
such that

x\in[\mathrm{BIN}_{\rho\cdot j+1:\rho\cdot(j+1)}(u),\mathrm{BIN}_{\rho\cdot j+1:\rho\cdot(j+1)}(u)+1-\eta],

then the network satisfies

F\left(\begin{pmatrix}x\\
w\\
u\end{pmatrix}\right)=\mathrm{BIN}_{c\cdot j+1:c\cdot(j+1)}(w)~.

Moreover,
F\left(\begin{pmatrix}x\\
w\\
u\end{pmatrix}\right)=0
for

x\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\bigcup_{j\in\{0,\cdots,n-1\}}(\mathrm{BIN}_{\rho\cdot j+1:\rho\cdot(j+1)}(u)-\eta,\mathrm{BIN}_{\rho\cdot j+1:\rho\cdot(j+1)}(u)+1).

Proof.

We define the triangle function
\varphi(z):=\sigma(\sigma(2z)-\sigma(4z-2))
as introduced by
Telgarsky 2016
. For
i\in\{0,1,\dots,n-1\}
, we construct a network block
F_{i}
:

F_{i}:\begin{pmatrix}x\\
\varphi^{(i\cdot\rho)}\left(\frac{u}{2^{n\cdot\rho}}+\frac{1}{2^{n\cdot\rho+1}}\right)\\
\varphi^{(i\cdot\rho)}\left(\frac{u}{2^{n\cdot\rho}}+\frac{1}{2^{n\cdot\rho+2}}\right)\\
\varphi^{(i\cdot c)}\left(\frac{w}{2^{n\cdot c}}+\frac{1}{2^{n\cdot c+1}}\right)\\
\varphi^{(i\cdot c)}\left(\frac{w}{2^{n\cdot c}}+\frac{1}{2^{n\cdot c+2}}\right)\\
y\end{pmatrix}\mapsto\begin{pmatrix}x\\
\varphi^{((i+1)\cdot\rho)}\left(\frac{u}{2^{n\cdot\rho}}+\frac{1}{2^{n\cdot\rho+1}}\right)\\
\varphi^{((i+1)\cdot\rho)}\left(\frac{u}{2^{n\cdot\rho}}+\frac{1}{2^{n\cdot\rho}+2}\right)\\
\varphi^{((i+1)\cdot c)}\left(\frac{w}{2^{n\cdot c}}+\frac{1}{2^{n\cdot c+1}}\right)\\
\varphi^{((i+1)\cdot c)}\left(\frac{w}{2^{n\cdot c}}+\frac{1}{2^{n\cdot c+2}}\right)\\
y+y_{i}\end{pmatrix},

where

\displaystyle y_{i}=\begin{cases}\mathrm{BIN}_{i\cdot c+1:(i+1)\cdot c}(w)&\textrm{ if }x\in[\mathrm{BIN}_{i\cdot\rho+1:(i+1)\cdot\rho}(u),~\mathrm{BIN}_{i\cdot\rho+1:(i+1)\cdot\rho}(u)+1-\eta],\\
0&\textrm{ if }x\leq\mathrm{BIN}_{i\cdot\rho+1:(i+1)\cdot\rho}(u)-\eta\textrm{ or }x\geq\mathrm{BIN}_{i\cdot\rho+1:(i+1)\cdot\rho}(u)+1.\end{cases}

To compute
y_{i}
, we first extract the relevant bit segments from
u
and
w
using
Lemma

B.8
. We define two subnetworks
F_{i}^{w},F_{i}^{u}
:

\displaystyle F_{i}^{u}:\begin{pmatrix}\varphi^{(i\cdot\rho)}\left(\frac{u}{2^{n\cdot\rho}}+\frac{1}{2^{n\cdot\rho+1}}\right)\\
\varphi^{(i\cdot\rho)}\left(\frac{u}{2^{n\cdot\rho}}+\frac{1}{2^{n\cdot\rho+2}}\right)\end{pmatrix}\mapsto\begin{pmatrix}\varphi^{((i+1)\cdot\rho)}\left(\frac{u}{2^{n\cdot\rho}}+\frac{1}{2^{n\cdot\rho+1}}\right)\\
\varphi^{((i+1)\cdot\rho)}\left(\frac{u}{2^{n\cdot\rho}}+\frac{1}{2^{n\cdot\rho+2}}\right)\\
\mathrm{BIN}_{i\cdot\rho+1:(i+1)\cdot\rho}(u)\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle F_{i}^{w}:\begin{pmatrix}\varphi^{(i\cdot c)}\left(\frac{w}{2^{n\cdot c}}+\frac{1}{2^{n\cdot c+1}}\right)\\
\varphi^{(i\cdot c)}\left(\frac{w}{2^{n\cdot c}}+\frac{1}{2^{n\cdot c+2}}\right)\end{pmatrix}\mapsto\begin{pmatrix}\varphi^{((i+1)\cdot c)}\left(\frac{w}{2^{n\cdot c}}+\frac{1}{2^{n\cdot c+1}}\right)\\
\varphi^{((i+1)\cdot c)}\left(\frac{w}{2^{n\cdot c}}+\frac{1}{2^{n\cdot c+2}}\right)\\
\mathrm{BIN}_{i\cdot c+1:(i+1)\cdot c}(w)\end{pmatrix}~.

A subnetwork
F_{i}^{u}
maps the pair of triangle encodings of
u
to the updated encodings for
i+1
, along with the extracted bits
\mathrm{BIN}_{i\cdot\rho+1:(i+1)\cdot\rho}(u)
. A subnetwork
F_{i}^{w}
does the same for
w
, yielding
\mathrm{BIN}_{i\cdot c+1:(i+1)\cdot c}(w)
.

We then construct a network with width 2 and depth 2 to obtain
y_{i}
from inputs
\mathrm{BIN}_{i\cdot\rho+1:(i+1)\cdot\rho}(u)
and
x
. Firstly, we use
Lemma

B.6
to construct a network that output
\tilde{y}_{i}
:

\tilde{y}_{i}=\begin{cases}1&\text{if }x\in[\mathrm{BIN}_{i\cdot\rho+1:(i+1)\cdot\rho}(u),~\mathrm{BIN}_{i\cdot\rho+1:(i+1)\cdot\rho}(u)+1-\eta],\\
0&\text{if }x\leq\mathrm{BIN}_{i\cdot\rho+1:(i+1)\cdot\rho}(u)-\eta\text{ or }x\geq\mathrm{BIN}_{i\cdot\rho+1:(i+1)\cdot\rho}(u)+1.\end{cases}

Secondly, we construct the following
1
-layer network that use
\tilde{y}_{i}
as input:

\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{y}_{i}\\
\mathrm{BIN}_{i\cdot c+1:(i+1)\cdot c}(w)\end{pmatrix}\mapsto\sigma\left(\tilde{y}_{i}\cdot 2^{c+1}-2^{c+1}+\mathrm{BIN}_{i\cdot c+1:(i+1)\cdot c}(w)\right).

This ensures that the output is
\mathrm{BIN}_{i\cdot c+1:(i+1)\cdot c}(w)
if
\tilde{y}_{i}=1
, and the output is
0
if
\tilde{y}_{i}=0
since
\mathrm{BIN}_{i\cdot c+1:(i+1)\cdot c}(w)\leq 2^{c}
.

Finally, the full network
F
is constructed as a composition:

F:=G\circ F_{n-1}\circ\cdots\circ F_{0}\circ H~,

where for
x,w,u>0
:

(1)
H:\mathbb{R}^{3}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{6}
is a 1-layer network that maps
(x,w,u)
to the required initial encoding inputs, namely:

H:\begin{pmatrix}x\\
w\\
u\end{pmatrix}\mapsto\begin{pmatrix}x\\
\frac{u}{2^{n\cdot\rho}}+\frac{1}{2^{n\cdot\rho+1}}\\
\frac{u}{2^{n\cdot\rho}}+\frac{1}{2^{n\cdot\rho+2}}\\
\frac{w}{2^{n\cdot c}}+\frac{1}{2^{n\cdot c+1}}\\
\frac{w}{2^{n\cdot c}}+\frac{1}{2^{n\cdot c+2}}\\
0\end{pmatrix},

(2)
G:\mathbb{R}^{6}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
is a 1-layer network that outputs the last coordinate.

We verify the correctness of the construction. The output of the full network is given by:

F\left(\begin{pmatrix}x\\
w\\
u\end{pmatrix}\right)=\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}y_{i}.

If there exists
j\in\{0,1\dots,n-1\}
such that
x\in[\mathrm{BIN}_{\rho\cdot j+1:\rho\cdot(j+1)}(u),\mathrm{BIN}_{\rho\cdot j+1:\rho\cdot(j+1)}(u)+1-\eta]
, then by the construction we obtain
y_{j}=\mathrm{BIN}_{c\cdot j+1:c\cdot(j+1)}(w)
, while
y_{\ell}=0
for all
\ell\neq j
. This is because the bit-encoded intervals are disjoint as
\mathrm{BIN}_{\rho\cdot\ell+1:\rho\cdot(\ell+1)}(u)\neq\mathrm{BIN}_{\rho\cdot k+1:\rho\cdot(k+1)}(u)
. Hence, the final output of
F
is:

\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}y_{i}=y_{j}=\mathrm{BIN}_{c\cdot j+1:c\cdot(j+1)}(w).

We now analyze the width and depth of the constructed network
F
. Each block
F_{i}
comprises
F_{i}^{w}
and
F_{i}^{u}
, each of width 5. In addition, two neurons are used to process
x
and
y
, resulting in a total width of 12. The outputs
\tilde{y}_{i}
and
y_{i}
are produced by additional layers with width 2 and 1, respectively, both of which are smaller than
12
. We also compose the networks
H
and
G
, with width 6 and 1, respectively, again remaining within
12
.

Each of the networks
F_{i}^{u}
and
F_{i}^{w}
has depth at most
3\max\{\rho,c\}
. The layers obtaining
\tilde{y}_{i}
and
y_{i}
contribute an additional 2 layers, resulting in a total depth of
3\max\{\rho,c\}+2
for each block
F_{i}
. Composing all
n
such blocks, and including one additional layer each for
H
and
G
, the total depth of the network
F
is
3n\cdot\max\{\rho,c\}+2n+2
.

The bit complexity of
F_{i}^{u}
,
F_{i}^{w}
and
H
is bounded by
n\max\{\rho,c\}+2
, and all other parts of the network require less bit complexity. Hence, the bit complexity of
F
is bounded by
n\max\{\rho,c\}+2
.
∎

B.2.2
Precise Control of Robust Memorization Error

Lemma

B.13
constructs the network for Stage II in
Theorem

B.5
, while the robust memorization error is controlled in
Lemma

B.11
.

Lemma B.10
.

Let
N,C\in\mathbb{N}
, and let

(m_{1},y_{1}),\dots,(m_{N},y_{N})\in{\bm{D}}_{1,N,C}\subset\mathbb{N}\times[C]

be a set of
N
labeled samples with
m_{i}\neq m_{j}
for every
i\neq j
.
Then, there exists a neural network
F:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
with width
12
, depth
\tilde{O}(\sqrt{N})
,
\tilde{O}(\sqrt{N})
parameters and bit complexity
\tilde{O}(\sqrt{N})
such that

\displaystyle F(m)=\begin{cases}y_{i}\text{\hskip 10.22217ptfor every }m=m_{i}\text{ with }i\in[N],\\
0\text{\;\hskip 10.22217ptfor every }m\in\mathbb{N}\backslash\{m_{i}\}_{i\in[N]}.\end{cases}

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{M}=\{m_{i}\}_{i\in[N]}
. We group the elements in
\mathcal{M}
to
\lceil\sqrt{N}\rceil
groups, each containing at most
\lfloor\sqrt{N}\rfloor+1
natural numbers inside.
For each interval indexed by
j\in\{1,\dots,\lceil\sqrt{N}\rceil\}
, we define two integers
w_{j},u_{j}\in\mathbb{N}
to encode the integer
m_{i}\in\mathcal{M}
and the corresponding labels
y_{i}
as follows.

For each
i\in[N]
, letting
j:=\left\lceil\frac{i}{\lfloor\sqrt{N}\rfloor+1}\right\rceil
,
k:=i\mod(\lfloor\sqrt{N}\rfloor+1)

and
R:=max_{i\in[N]}\>m_{i}
, we define:

\displaystyle\mathrm{BIN}

{}_{k\cdot\log_{2}R+1:(k+1)\cdot\log_{2}R}(u_{j})=m_{i}

\displaystyle\mathrm{BIN}

{}_{k\cdot\log_{2}C+1:(k+1)\cdot\log_{2}C}(w_{j})=y_{i}~.

Thus, in each group
j
, the integer
u_{j}
contains
\log_{2}R
bits per integer, which represent the
k
-th integer in this group. In the same manner,
w_{j}
contains
\log_{2}C
bits per integer, which represent the label of the
k
-th integer in this group.

By applying
Lemma

B.7
to
\eta=\frac{1}{2}
, we construct a neural network
F_{1}
that maps
m\in\mathcal{M}
to their corresponding groups, and maps
m\in\mathbb{N}\backslash\bigcup_{j\in[\lceil\sqrt{N}\rceil]}[m_{(j-1)(\lfloor\sqrt{N}\rfloor+1)+1},\;m_{j(\lfloor\sqrt{N}\rfloor+1)}+1)
to
0
.
Thus, all natural numbers are assigned to their corresponding group or
0
.

For each
i\in[N]
, we define the group index

j_{i}:=\left\lceil\frac{i}{\lfloor\sqrt{N}\rfloor+1}\right\rceil.

Then, the network
F_{1}
maps any input
m\in\mathcal{M}
to the representation

F_{1}(m)=\begin{pmatrix}m\\
w_{j_{i}}\\
u_{j_{i}}\end{pmatrix},

and
F_{1}(m)=\begin{pmatrix}m\\
0\\
0\end{pmatrix}
for
m\in\mathbb{N}\backslash\bigcup_{j\in[\lceil\sqrt{N}\rceil]}[m_{(j-1)(\lfloor\sqrt{N}\rfloor+1)+1},\;m_{j(\lfloor\sqrt{N}\rfloor+1)}+1)
.
The network
F_{1}
has width 9, depth
\tilde{O}(\sqrt{N})
and bit complexity
\tilde{O}(1)
.

Now, we apply
Lemma

B.9
to construct a network
F_{2}:\mathbb{R}^{3}\to\mathbb{R}
with the following property. For each
i\in[N],j\in\left[\lceil\sqrt{N}\rceil\right]
, and
k\in\left\{0,\ldots,\lfloor\sqrt{N}\rfloor\right\}
, suppose that
m_{i}
is the
k
-th integer in the
j
-th group. Then, the network satisfies :

F_{2}\left(\begin{pmatrix}m_{i}\\
w_{j}\\
u_{j}\end{pmatrix}\right)=\mathrm{BIN}_{k\cdot\log_{2}C+1:(k+1)\cdot\log_{2}C}(w_{j})=y_{i}.

Moreover, for
m\in\mathbb{N}\setminus\mathcal{M}
,
F_{2}\left(\begin{pmatrix}m\\
w_{j}\\
u_{j}\end{pmatrix}\right)=0
or
F_{2}\left(\begin{pmatrix}m\\
0\\
0\end{pmatrix}\right)=0
.
Thus, the network
F_{2}
extracts the label corresponding to each data point from the encoded label set of the group to which the interval belongs or outputs
0
.
The network
F_{2}
has width 12, depth
\tilde{O}(\sqrt{N})
and bit complexity
\tilde{O}(\sqrt{N})
.

Finally, we define the classifier network
F:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
as

F({\bm{x}})=F_{2}\circ F_{1}({\bm{x}}).

The overall network
F
has width
12
and depth
\tilde{O}(\sqrt{N})
, which corresponds to the maximum width and total depth of its component networks. The bit complexity of
F
is
\tilde{O}(\sqrt{N})
.
∎

Lemma B.11
.

Let
\mathcal{B}_{2}({\bm{x}}_{0},\mu)
be a Euclidean ball with center
{\bm{x}}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and radius
\mu>0
.
Let
{\bm{u}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
be a unit vector, and define the affine function
f({\bm{x}}):=\frac{1}{2\mu}({\bm{u}}^{\top}{\bm{x}}+b)
for some
b\in\mathbb{R}
. Then for any interval
I\subset\mathbb{R}
of length
\eta
, the volume fraction of the ball mapped into
I
satisfies:

\frac{\operatorname{Vol}\left(\{x\in\mathcal{B}_{2}(x_{0},\mu)\,\middle|\,f(x)\in I\}\right)}{\operatorname{Vol}\left(\mathcal{B}_{2}(x_{0},\mu)\right)}\leq{2\eta}\frac{V_{d-1}}{V_{d}},

where
V_{d}=\frac{\pi^{d/2}}{\Gamma(\frac{d}{2}+1)}
denotes the volume of the
d
-dimensional unit ball.

Proof.

Let
{\bm{x}}={\bm{x}}_{0}+\mu{\bm{y}}
, so that
{\bm{y}}\in\mathcal{B}_{d}({\bm{0}},1)
. Under this change of variables,

f({\bm{x}})=\frac{1}{2\mu}({\bm{u}}^{\top}({\bm{x}}_{0}+\mu{\bm{y}})+b)=\frac{1}{2}({\bm{u}}^{\top}{\bm{y}})+\frac{1}{2\mu}({\bm{u}}^{\top}{\bm{x}}_{0}+b).

Thus,
f({\bm{x}})\in I
if and only if
{\bm{u}}^{\top}{\bm{y}}\in J
, where

J:=2I-\frac{1}{\mu}({\bm{u}}^{\top}{\bm{x}}_{0}+b)\subset\mathbb{R}

is an interval of length
2\eta
.
We define the preimage of
I
under
f
with the intersection of
{\mathcal{B}}_{2}({\bm{x}}_{0},\mu)
as

A:=\left\{x\in\mathcal{B}_{2}({\bm{x}}_{0},\mu)\,\middle|\,f({\bm{x}})\in I\right\}.

Then,

\operatorname{Vol}(A)=\mu^{d}\cdot\operatorname{Vol}\left(\left\{y\in\mathcal{B}_{d}({\bm{0}},1)\middle|{\bm{u}}^{\top}{\bm{y}}\in J\right\}\right).

The distribution of
{\bm{u}}^{\top}{\bm{y}}
, where
{\bm{y}}\sim\operatorname{Unif}(\mathcal{B}_{2}({\bm{0}},1))
, has density

p(t)=\frac{V_{d-1}}{V_{d}}(1-t^{2})^{\frac{d-1}{2}}\text{ for }t\in[-1,1],\quad\textrm{ where }V_{d}=\frac{\pi^{d/2}}{\Gamma(\frac{d}{2}+1)}.

Thus,

\displaystyle\operatorname{Vol}(A)

\displaystyle=\mu^{d}\cdot V_{d}\int_{J}p(t)dt\leq\mu^{d}\int_{J}{V_{d-1}}\,dt=2\eta\cdot\mu^{d}{V_{d-1}},

\displaystyle\operatorname{Vol}({\mathcal{B}}_{2}({\bm{x}}_{0},\mu))

\displaystyle=\mu^{d}V_{d}.

Hence,

\frac{\operatorname{Vol}\left({{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{B}_{2}({\bm{x}}_{0},\mu):f({\bm{x}})\in I}\right)}{\operatorname{Vol}\left(\mathcal{B}_{2}({\bm{x}}_{0},\mu)\right)}=\frac{\operatorname{Vol}(A)}{\operatorname{Vol}({\mathcal{B}}_{2}({\bm{x}}_{0},\mu))}\leq{2\eta}\frac{V_{d-1}}{V_{d}}.

∎

Lemma B.12
.

For all integers
d\geq 1
,

\frac{V_{d}}{V_{d-1}}\leq 2,

where
V_{d}=\dfrac{\pi^{d/2}}{\Gamma\!\left(\frac{d}{2}+1\right)}
is the volume of the
d
-dimensional unit ball.

Proof.

Set

R_{d}:=\frac{V_{d}}{V_{d-1}}=\sqrt{\pi}\,\frac{\Gamma\!\left(\frac{d+1}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\!\left(\frac{d}{2}+1\right)}.

Let
x=\tfrac{d}{2}
and define

g(x):=\log R_{2x}=\tfrac{1}{2}\log\pi+\log\Gamma\!\left(x+\tfrac{1}{2}\right)-\log\Gamma(x+1).

Differentiating and using the digamma function
\psi=\Gamma^{\prime}/\Gamma
, we get

g^{\prime}(x)=\psi\!\left(x+\tfrac{1}{2}\right)-\psi(x+1)<0,

since
\psi
is strictly increasing. Hence
R_{d}
is strictly decreasing in
d
.
Therefore
\max_{d\geq 1}R_{d}=R_{1}=V_{1}/V_{0}=2
, which proves
R_{d}\leq 2
with equality only at
d=1
.
∎

Lemma B.13
.

Let
\eta\in(0,1)
,
\alpha\in[0,1]
and
{\mathcal{D}}=\{({\bm{x}}_{i},y_{i})\}_{i\in[N]}\in{\bm{D}}_{d,N,C}
be a dataset with separation
\epsilon_{\mathcal{D}}\geq\sqrt{d}/2
, and let the robustness ratio be
\rho=\frac{1}{4\lfloor N^{\alpha}\rfloor\epsilon_{\mathcal{D}}}
. Then, for any index set
I\subseteq[N]
with
|I|\leq\lfloor N^{\alpha}\rfloor+1
, there exists a neural network
f
with width
O(d)
, depth
\tilde{O}(N^{\frac{\alpha}{2}})
,
\tilde{O}\left(N^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}+d^{2}\right)
parameters and
\tilde{O}(N^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}+d)
bit complexity such that:

\displaystyle f({\bm{x}})

\displaystyle=y_{i}\quad

\displaystyle\forall

\displaystyle i\in I,{\bm{x}}\in{\mathcal{B}}({\bm{x}}_{i},\rho\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}}),

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{{\bm{x}}\in\operatorname{Unif}({\mathcal{B}}({\bm{x}}_{i},\rho\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}}))}\left[f({\bm{x}})\in\{0,y_{i}\}\right]

\displaystyle\geq 1-\eta\quad

\displaystyle\forall

\displaystyle i\in[N]\backslash I.

A network
f
, obtained from this lemma, memorizes each data point and its robustness ball for all indices
i\in I
.
f
maps every other data point and its robustness ball to either its correct label or
0
with high probability
1-\eta
.

Proof.

We construct a network proceeding in three stages. In each stage, we define subnetworks such that their composition satisfies the requirements.

Stage I
(Translation for Distancing from Lattice via the Bias)
We first translate the data points so that for
i\in I
, the robustness ball centered at
{\bm{x}}_{i}
lies far from integer lattice boundaries. This ensures that each ball lies entirely within a single unit grid cell.
By applying
Lemma

B.15
to the points
\{{\bm{x}}_{i}\}_{i\in I}
, we obtain a translation vector
{\bm{b}}=(b_{1},\cdots,b_{d})\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
with bit complexity
\lceil\log(6|I|)\rceil
such that

\displaystyle\mathrm{dist}(x_{i,j}-b_{j},\mathbb{Z})\geq\frac{1}{3\lfloor N^{\alpha}\rfloor},\quad\forall i\in I,j\in[d],

(24)

i.e., the translated points
\{{\bm{x}}_{i}-{\bm{b}}\}_{i\in I}
are coordinate-wise far from the integer lattice.
Additionally, we apply an integer-valued translation (coordinate-wise) so that all coordinates of the points
\{{\bm{x}}_{i}-{\bm{b}}\}_{i\in[N]}
become positive, while preserving the distance property in
Equation

24
.
Hence, without loss of generality, we can assume
{\bm{b}}
also has the property

\displaystyle{\bm{x}}_{i}-{\bm{b}}\geq{\bm{0}}\textrm{ for all }i\in[N].

(25)

Let
{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime}:=\{({\bm{x}}_{i}^{\prime},y_{i})\}_{i\in[N]}
, where
{\bm{x}}_{i}^{\prime}:={\bm{x}}_{i}-{\bm{b}}
.
Then
\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime}}=\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}}
.
For
\rho^{\prime}:=\rho=\frac{1}{4\lfloor N^{\alpha}\rfloor\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}}}
, we have the robustness radius
\mu^{\prime}:=\rho^{\prime}\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime}}=\rho\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}}=\frac{1}{4\lfloor N^{\alpha}\rfloor}
.
Define
f_{\mathrm{trans}}
as
f_{\mathrm{trans}}({\bm{x}}):={\bm{x}}-{\bm{b}}
.
Then,
f_{\mathrm{trans}}
can be implemented via one hidden layer with
O(d^{2})
parameters in a neural network.

Since the translation preserves separation (
\epsilon_{\mathcal{D}}=\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime}}
) and ball containment properties (robustness ball of
{\mathcal{D}}
is mapped to the robustness ball of
{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime}
through the translation), it suffices to construct a network that satisfies the requirements with
\rho\leftarrow\rho^{\prime}
and
{\mathcal{D}}\leftarrow{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime}
.
Observe that the robustness balls after Stage I are not affected when passing the
{\sigma}
, by
Equations

24
and
25
.

Stage II
(Grid Indexing)
From
Equation

24
, each
{\bm{x}}_{i}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
(for
i\in I
) is at least
\frac{4}{3}\mu^{\prime}
distant from any lattice hyperplane
H_{z,j}:=\{{\bm{x}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\mid x_{j}=z\}
for each
j\in[d]
and
z\in\mathbb{Z}
.
Hence, each robustness ball centered at
{\bm{x}}_{i}^{\prime}
(for
i\in I
) lies completely within a single integer lattice (or unit grid)
\prod_{j=1}^{d}[n_{j},n_{j}+1)
, for some
(n_{1},\cdots,n_{d})\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}
. Moreover, for any
{\bm{x}}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{2}({\bm{x}}_{i}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
, the distance from the integer lattice remains at least
\mu^{\prime}
.

Furthermore, by the separation condition
\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime}}=\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}}\geq\sqrt{d}/2
, for any
i\neq i^{\prime}
with
y_{i}\neq y_{i^{\prime}}
, we have
\left\lVert{\bm{x}}_{i}^{\prime}-{\bm{x}}_{i^{\prime}}^{\prime}\right\rVert_{2}\geq\sqrt{d}
.
Since
\sup\{\left\lVert{\bm{x}}-{\bm{x}}^{\prime}\right\rVert_{2}\,\mid\,{\bm{x}},{\bm{x}}^{\prime}\in\prod_{j=1}^{d}[n_{j},n_{j}+1)\}=\sqrt{d}
, two such points cannot lie in the same grid.
Recall the separation condition holds for all data points
{\bm{x}}_{i}^{\prime}
for
i\in[N]
and each ball
{\mathcal{B}}_{2}({\bm{x}}_{i}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
(for
{i\in I}
) lies within a single grid. We conclude that for each
i\in I
, the robustness ball
\mathcal{B}_{2}({\bm{x}}_{i}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
is not intersected by any other robustness ball
\mathcal{B}_{2}({\bm{x}}_{j}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
with a different label, for any
j\in[N]
, i.e., no ball with a different label overlaps the grid cell containing
\mathcal{B}_{2}({\bm{x}}_{i}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
.

We define
R:=\lceil\max_{i\in I}\left\lVert{\bm{x}}_{i}^{\prime}\right\rVert_{\infty}(=\max_{i\in I,j\in[d]}(x_{i,j}^{\prime}))\rceil\in{\mathbb{N}}
.
Our goal in this stage is to construct
\mathrm{Flatten}
mapping defined as

\mathrm{Flatten}({\bm{x}}):=R^{d-1}\lfloor x_{1}\rfloor+R^{d-2}\lfloor x_{2}\rfloor+\cdots+\lfloor x_{d}\rfloor.

This maps each grid
\prod_{j=1}^{d}[n_{j},n_{j+1})
onto the point
\sum_{j=1}^{d}R^{j-1}n_{j}
.

However, since
\mathrm{Flatten}
is discontinuous due to the use of floor functions, we construct
\overline{\mathrm{Flatten}}
which is a continuous approximation that exactly matches
\mathrm{Flatten}
on the region
\bigcup_{i\in I}{\mathcal{B}}_{2}({\bm{x}}_{i}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
, and incurs only a small error on the remaining region
\bigcup_{i\in[N]\setminus I}{\mathcal{B}}_{2}({\bm{x}}_{i}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
.
We choose large enough
t\in{\mathbb{N}}
so that for
\eta^{\prime}:=1/t
, we have
\eta^{\prime}\leq\frac{V_{d}}{2dV_{d-1}}\mu^{\prime}\eta
where
V_{d}=\frac{\pi^{d/2}}{\Gamma(\frac{d}{2}+1)}
denotes the volume of the
d
-dimensional unit ball.
Moreover, we can take such
t\in{\mathbb{N}}
which at the same time satisfies

\displaystyle t\leq\frac{2dV_{d-1}}{V_{d}\mu^{\prime}\eta}+1=\frac{2d\Gamma(\frac{d}{2}+1)}{\pi^{1/2}\Gamma(\frac{d-1}{2}+1)\mu^{\prime}\eta}+1=O(d^{2}/(\mu^{\prime}\eta))=O(d^{2}\lfloor N^{\alpha}\rfloor/\eta).

By
Lemma

B.16
, for
\gamma:=1/t=\eta^{\prime}
and
n:=\lceil\log_{2}R\rceil
, we obtain the network
\overline{{\mathrm{Floor}}}:=\overline{{\mathrm{Floor}}_{\lceil\log_{2}R\rceil}}
with
O(\log_{2}R)
parameters such that

\displaystyle\overline{{\mathrm{Floor}}}(x)=\lfloor x\rfloor\quad\forall x\in[0,R]\textrm{ with }x-\lfloor x\rfloor>\eta^{\prime}.

(26)

Since we apply
\gamma=1/t
to
Lemma

B.16
,
\overline{{\mathrm{Floor}}}
can be implemented with
O(n+\log t)=O(\log R+\log(d^{2}\lfloor N^{\alpha}\rfloor/\eta))=O(\log(dRN/\eta))
bit complexity.
In particular, we can define our network
\overline{\mathrm{Flatten}}
with
O(\log(dRN/\eta)+\log R^{d-1})=O(\log(dRN/\eta)+d\log R)=\tilde{O}(d)
bit complexity as

\displaystyle\overline{\mathrm{Flatten}}({\bm{x}})=R^{d-1}\overline{{\mathrm{Floor}}}(x_{1})+\cdots+\overline{{\mathrm{Floor}}}(x_{d}).

(27)

As
\overline{\mathrm{Floor}}:{\mathbb{R}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}
can be implemented with width 5 and depth
O(\log_{2}R)
network (
Lemma

B.16
),
\overline{\mathrm{Flatten}}
can be implemented with width
5d
and depth
O(\log_{2}R)
network.
Thus, we can construct
\overline{\mathrm{Flatten}}
with
O(d^{2}\log_{2}R)=\tilde{O}(d^{2})
parameters.

We first observe that this implementation is valid on the region
\bigcup_{i\in I}{\mathcal{B}}_{2}({\bm{x}}_{i}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
.
For
i\in I
and
{\bm{x}}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{2}({\bm{x}}_{i}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
, we have

\displaystyle x_{j}-\lfloor x_{j}\rfloor\overset{(a)}{>}\mu^{\prime}\overset{(b)}{>}\mu^{\prime}\eta\overset{(c)}{\geq}\frac{V_{d}}{2V_{d-1}}\mu^{\prime}\eta>\frac{V_{d}}{2dV_{d-1}}\mu^{\prime}\eta\overset{(d)}{\geq}\eta^{\prime},

where (a) holds by
Equation

24
, (b) holds since
\eta<1
, (c) holds since
\frac{V_{d}}{V_{d-1}}\leq 2
by
Lemma

B.12
, and (d) holds from the choice of
\eta^{\prime}
.
Thus, for any
i\in I
and any
{\bm{x}}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{2}({\bm{x}}_{i}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
,
x_{j}
satisfies the requirement in
Equation

26
. Therefore, we guarantee that each robustness ball centered at
{\bm{x}}^{\prime}_{i}
for
{i\in I}
lies in the region where the
\mathrm{Flatten}
is properly approximated by
\overline{\mathrm{Flatten}}
. i.e.

\displaystyle\overline{\mathrm{Flatten}}({\bm{x}})=\mathrm{Flatten}({\bm{x}})\textrm{ for all }i\in I\textrm{ and }{\bm{x}}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{2}({\bm{x}}_{i}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime}).

Since
\mathrm{Flatten}
maps each unit grid into a point and each robustness ball centered at
{\bm{x}}^{\prime}_{i}
for
{i\in I}
lies on a single unit grid, we conclude

\displaystyle\overline{\mathrm{Flatten}}({\bm{x}})=\mathrm{Flatten}({\bm{x}})=\mathrm{Flatten}({\bm{x}}_{i}^{\prime})\textrm{ for all }i\in I\textrm{ and }{\bm{x}}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{2}({\bm{x}}_{i}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime}).

Let
m_{i}:=\mathrm{Flatten}({\bm{x}}_{i}^{\prime})
for
i\in I
.
Then for
i\in I
, each robustness ball centered at
{\bm{x}}_{i}^{\prime}
is mapped to
m_{i}
. We have
m_{i}\in~{\mathbb{Z}}\cap[0,R^{d+1}]
for all
i\in I
, since

\displaystyle m_{i}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Flatten}({\bm{x}}_{i}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=R^{d-1}\lfloor x_{i1}^{\prime}\rfloor+R^{d-2}\lfloor x_{i2}^{\prime}\rfloor+\cdots\lfloor x_{id}^{\prime}\rfloor

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}R^{d-1}R+R^{d-2}R+\cdots+R

\displaystyle\leq R^{d+1},

where (a) is by
\left\lVert{\bm{x}}_{i}^{\prime}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq R
.

Next, we consider the case
i\in[N]\setminus I
.
Note that the lattice distance condition in
Equation

24
applies only to the subset
\{({\bm{x}}_{i},y_{i})\}_{i\in I}
, rather than the entire dataset. As a result, for indices
i\in[N]\setminus I
, the distance from the lattice is not guaranteed. Thus, it can lie across the lattice.

For
i\in[N]\setminus I
, we analyze the error of
\overline{\mathrm{Flatten}}
on the remaining region
\bigcup_{i\in[N]\setminus I}{\mathcal{B}}_{2}({\bm{x}}_{i}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
. For
i\in[N]\setminus I
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{{\bm{x}}\in\operatorname{Unif}({\mathcal{B}}_{2}({\bm{x}}_{i}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime}))}\left[\overline{\mathrm{Flatten}}({\bm{x}})\neq\mathrm{Flatten}({\bm{x}})\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{{\bm{x}}\in\operatorname{Unif}({\mathcal{B}}_{2}({\bm{x}}_{i}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime}))}\left[\exists j\in[d],\quad x_{j}-\lfloor x_{j}\rfloor\leq\eta^{\prime}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in[d]}\mathbb{P}_{{\bm{x}}\in\operatorname{Unif}({\mathcal{B}}({\bm{x}}_{i}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime}))}\left[x_{j}-\lfloor x_{j}\rfloor\leq\eta^{\prime}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in[d]}\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathcal{I}\subseteq{\mathbb{R}}\\
\text{s.t. }\operatorname{Len}(\mathcal{I})=\eta^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}_{{\bm{x}}\in\operatorname{Unif}({\mathcal{B}}({\bm{x}}_{i}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime}))}\left[x_{j}\in\mathcal{I}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\leq}

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in[d]}\frac{2\eta^{\prime}V_{d-1}}{\mu^{\prime}V_{d}}\quad

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2\eta^{\prime}dV_{d-1}}{\mu^{\prime}V_{d}}

\displaystyle\overset{(c)}{\leq}

\displaystyle\eta,

where (a) follows from
Equation

26
and the fact that
\overline{\mathrm{Flatten}}({\bm{x}})=\mathrm{Flatten}({\bm{x}})
whenever
x_{j}-\lfloor x_{j}\rfloor>\eta^{\prime}
for all
j\in[d]
, (b) follows from
Lemma

B.11
applied to a unit vector
u=\mathbf{e_{j}}
,
b=0
, and an interval
\frac{\mathcal{I}_{j}}{\mu^{\prime}}
, and (c) holds by the choice of
\eta^{\prime}
.
Hence, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{{\bm{x}}\in\operatorname{Unif}({\mathcal{B}}_{2}({\bm{x}}_{i}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime}))}\left[\overline{\mathrm{Flatten}}({\bm{x}})\neq\mathrm{Flatten}({\bm{x}})\right]\leq\eta.

(28)

We observe at what happens if
\overline{\mathrm{Flatten}}({\bm{x}})=\mathrm{Flatten}({\bm{x}})
for
i\in[N]\setminus I
and
{\bm{x}}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{2}({\bm{x}}_{i}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
.
To ensure that no robustness ball centered at
{\bm{x}}_{i}
for
i\in[N]\setminus I
is mapped to grid index
m_{j}
with a different label, namely, satisfying
j\in I
with
y_{i}\neq y_{j}
, we define label-specific grid index sets.
For each class
c\in[C]
, define the set

G_{c}:=\bigcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in I\\
\text{s.t. }y_{i}=c\end{subarray}}\{m_{i}\},\text{ and }G:=\bigcup_{c\in[C]}G_{c}=\{m_{i}\}_{i\in[N]},

where
G_{c}
is the collection of all grid indices
m_{i}
assigned to data points in
I
that have label
y_{i}=c
.
In other words,
G_{c}
contains all grid cells that are claimed by class
c
.
The set
G
represents all valid grid indices.

Recall that for each
i\in I
, the robustness ball
\mathcal{B}_{2}({\bm{x}}_{i}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
is not intersected by any other robustness ball
\mathcal{B}_{2}({\bm{x}}_{j}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
with a different label.
Specifically, consider
i\in[N]\setminus I
.
For
j\in I
with
y_{i}=y_{j}
, the robustness ball
\mathcal{B}_{2}({\bm{x}}_{i}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
can have a portion that intersects the grid containing
\mathcal{B}_{2}({\bm{x}}_{j}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
, then the portion is mapped to the corresponding grid index
m_{j}
.
However, for
j\in I
with
y_{i}\neq y_{j}
, the robustness ball never intersects the grid, and is never mapped to
m_{j}
.
Formally, if
\mathrm{Flatten}({\bm{x}})\in G
, it must be
\mathrm{Flatten}({\bm{x}})\in G_{y_{i}}
. Otherwise,
\mathrm{Flatten}({\bm{x}})\notin G
, i.e., the robustness ball does not intersect any selected grid.
Thus, combining the probabilities,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{{\bm{x}}\in\operatorname{Unif}({\mathcal{B}}_{2}({\bm{x}}_{i}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime}))}\left[\overline{\mathrm{Flatten}}({\bm{x}})\in G_{y_{i}}\text{ or }\overline{\mathrm{Flatten}}({\bm{x}})\notin G\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\geq}

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{{\bm{x}}\in\operatorname{Unif}({\mathcal{B}}_{2}({\bm{x}}_{i}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime}))}\left[\overline{\mathrm{Flatten}}({\bm{x}})=\mathrm{Flatten}({\bm{x}})\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\geq}

\displaystyle 1-\eta,

(29)

where (a) holds since
\mathbb{P}_{{\bm{x}}\in\operatorname{Unif}({\mathcal{B}}_{2}({\bm{x}}_{i}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime}))}\left[{\mathrm{Flatten}}({\bm{x}})\in G_{y_{i}}\text{ or }{\mathrm{Flatten}}({\bm{x}})\notin G\right]=1
, and (b) follows by
Equation

28
.
Hence, if we memorize
\{(m_{i},y_{i})\}_{i\in I}
and map other integer
\mathbb{N}\setminus\{m_{i}\}_{i\in I}
to zero,
{\mathcal{B}}_{2}({\bm{x}}_{i}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
for
i\in I
is exactly mapped to
y_{i}
, and with high probability
1-\eta
,
{\mathcal{B}}_{2}({\bm{x}}_{i}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
for
i\in[N]\setminus I
is mapped to either
y_{i}
or
0
.

Stage III
(Memorization)
Finally, we construct the network to memorize
\lfloor N^{\alpha}\rfloor
points
\{(m_{i},y_{i})\}_{i=1}^{I}\subset{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}\times[C]
.
Since multiple robustness balls for
{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime}
with the same label may correspond to the same grid index in Stage II, it is possible that for some
i\neq j
with
y_{i}=y_{j}
, we have
m_{i}=m_{j}
.
Let
N^{\prime}\leq|I|
denote the number of distinct pairs
(m_{i},y_{i})
.
It remains to memorize these
N^{\prime}
distinct data points in
{\mathbb{R}}
.

Applying
Lemma

B.10
, we obtain a neural network
f_{\text{mem}}
with width 12, depth
\tilde{O}(N)
,
\tilde{O}(N^{\frac{\alpha}{2}})
parameters and bit complexity
\tilde{O}(N^{\frac{\alpha}{2}})
satisfying:

\displaystyle f_{\text{mem}}(m)=\begin{cases}y_{i}\text{\quad for every }m=m_{i}\text{ with }i\in I,\\
0\text{\;\quad for every }m\in\mathbb{N}\backslash G.\end{cases}

For
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
f_{\text{mem}}(m)=c
for some
c\in[C]
if and only if
m\in G_{c}
.

The final network
f:{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}
is defined as

\displaystyle f=f_{\mathrm{mem}}\circ{\sigma}\circ\overline{\mathrm{Flatten}}\circ{\sigma}\circ f_{\mathrm{trans}}.

Let us verify the correctness of the construction.

For
i\in I
and any
{\bm{x}}\in{\mathcal{B}}({\bm{x}}_{i},\rho\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}})
, we have

f({\bm{x}})=f_{\mathrm{mem}}\circ{\sigma}\circ\overline{\mathrm{Flatten}}\circ{\sigma}\circ f_{\mathrm{trans}}({\bm{x}})\overset{(a)}{=}f_{\mathrm{mem}}\circ{\sigma}(m_{i})\overset{(b)}{=}f_{\text{mem}}(m_{i})=y_{i},

where (a) holds since
\overline{\mathrm{Flatten}}\circ{\sigma}\circ f_{\mathrm{trans}}({\bm{x}}){=}m_{i}
, (b) holds since
m_{i}\in\mathbb{N}
, and (c) follows that
f
is constructed to memorize
\{(m_{i},y_{i})\}_{i=1}^{I}
.

Next, consider
i\in[N]\setminus I
. We observe

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{{\bm{x}}\in\operatorname{Unif}({\mathcal{B}}({\bm{x}}_{i},\rho\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}}))}\left[f({\bm{x}})\in\{0,y_{i}\}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{=}

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{{\bm{x}}\in\operatorname{Unif}({\mathcal{B}}_{2}({\bm{x}}_{i},\mu))}\left[{\sigma}\circ\overline{\mathrm{Flatten}}\circ{\sigma}\circ f_{\mathrm{trans}}({\bm{x}})\in G_{y_{i}}\text{ or }{\sigma}\circ\overline{\mathrm{Flatten}}\circ{\sigma}\circ f_{\mathrm{trans}}({\bm{x}})\notin G\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{=}

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{{\bm{x}}^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Unif}({\mathcal{B}}_{2}({\bm{x}}_{i}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime}))}\left[{\sigma}\circ\overline{\mathrm{Flatten}}({\bm{x}}^{\prime})\in G_{y_{i}}\text{ or }{\sigma}\circ\overline{\mathrm{Flatten}}({\bm{x}}^{\prime})\notin G\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(c)}{=}

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{{\bm{x}}^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Unif}({\mathcal{B}}_{2}({\bm{x}}_{i}^{\prime},\mu^{\prime}))}\left[\overline{\mathrm{Flatten}}({\bm{x}}^{\prime})\in G_{y_{i}}\text{ or }\overline{\mathrm{Flatten}}({\bm{x}}^{\prime})\notin G\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(d)}{\geq}

\displaystyle 1-\eta,

where (a) holds from the construction of
f_{\mathrm{mem}}
, (b) holds using
{\bm{x}}^{\prime}:={\sigma}\circ f_{\mathrm{trans}}({\bm{x}})
, (c) holds by
Equation

25
and (d) holds by
Equation

29
.
This concludes the proof.

The depth 1 network
f_{\mathrm{trans}}
has width
d
.
\overline{\mathrm{Flatten}}
has width
5d
and depth
O(\log_{2}R)
and
f_{\mathrm{mem}}
has width
12
and depth
\tilde{O}(N^{\frac{\alpha}{2}})
.
The total construction requires
\tilde{O}(d^{2}+d^{2}+N^{\frac{\alpha}{2}})=\tilde{O}(d^{2}+N^{\frac{\alpha}{2}})
parameters, where each term
d^{2},d^{2}
, and
N^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}
comes from
f_{\mathrm{trans}}
,
\overline{\mathrm{Flatten}}
, and
f_{\mathrm{mem}}
respectively.
The width of the final network is
{O}(d)
and the depth is
\tilde{O}(N^{\frac{\alpha}{2}})
.

The bit complexity of
f_{\mathrm{trans}}
is
O(\log N^{\alpha},\log(\max\{\|{\bm{x}}_{i}\|_{\infty}\mid i\in[N]\}))=\tilde{O}(1)
.
\overline{\mathrm{Flatten}}
has the bit complexity
\tilde{O}(d)
, and
f_{\mathrm{mem}}
needs at most
\tilde{O}(N^{\frac{\alpha}{2}})
.
Hence, the bit complexity of the final network is
\tilde{O}(N^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}+d)
.
∎

B.3
Sufficient Condition for Robust Memorization with Large Robustness Radius

Theorem B.14
.

Let
\rho\in\Big(\frac{1}{5\sqrt{d}},1\Big)
. For any dataset
{\mathcal{D}}\in{\bm{D}}_{d,N,C}
, there exists
f
with
\tilde{O}(Nd^{{2}}\rho^{{4}})
parameters, depth
\tilde{O}(N)
, width
\tilde{O}(\rho^{2}d)
and bit complexity
\tilde{O}({N})
that
\rho
-robustly memorizes
{\mathcal{D}}
.

Proof.

Let
{\mathcal{D}}=\{({\bm{x}}_{i},y_{i})\}_{i\in[N]}\in{\bm{D}}_{d,N,C}
be given.
We divide the proof into five cases, the first case under
\rho\in[1/3,1)
, the second case under
\rho\in(1/5\sqrt{d},1/3)
and
d<600\log N
, the third case under
\rho\in(1/5\sqrt{d},1/3)
and
N<600\log N\leq d
, the fourth case under
\rho\in(1/5\sqrt{d},1/3)
,
N\geq d\geq 600\log N
, and finally the fifth case under
\rho\in(1/5\sqrt{d},1/3)
and
d>N\geq 600\log N
.
To check that these cases cover all the cases, refer to
Figure

6
.

\rho\in\left[\frac{1}{3},1\right)?
Case I
d<600\log N?
Case II
N<600\log N?
Case III
d\leq N?
Case IV
Case V

Figure 6
:
Different cases for
Theorem

B.14
.
The left child is for the answer “Yes”, and the right child is for the answer “No”

The first two cases follow easily from prior works, while the remaining cases require careful analysis using dimension reduction techniques.
The most interesting cases are cases IV and V.
While we track the width, depth, and parameter complexity for each case, we initially implement them using infinite precision.
We address the bit complexity by approximating the infinite precision network using a finite precision network at the very last part of the proof.
As a spoiler, the bit complexity of all cases is handled within a unified framework using
Lemma

B.23
.
Let us deal with each case one by one.

Case I:
\rho\in[1/3,1)
.

In the first case, where
\rho\in[1/3,1)
, the result directly follows from the prior result by
Yu et al. 2024
.
In particular, we apply
Lemma

D.2
.
Let us denote
R:=\max_{i\in[N]}\left\lVert{\bm{x}}_{i}\right\rVert_{2}
and
\gamma:=(1-\rho)\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}}
.
Note that
R\geq\left\lVert{\bm{x}}_{i}\right\rVert_{\infty}
for all
i\in[N]
as
\left\lVert{\bm{x}}\right\rVert_{2}\geq\left\lVert{\bm{x}}\right\rVert_{\infty}
for all
{\bm{x}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
.
By applying
Lemma

D.2
, there exists
f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{d,P}
with
P=O(Nd^{2}(\log(\frac{d}{\gamma^{2}})+\log R))
parameters that
\rho
-robustly memorize
{\mathcal{D}}
.
The number of parameters can be further bounded as follows:

\displaystyle O(Nd^{2}(\log(\frac{d}{\gamma^{2}})+\log R))\overset{(a)}{=}O(Nd^{2}\rho^{4}\cdot(\log(\frac{d}{\gamma^{2}})+\log R))\overset{(b)}{=}\tilde{O}(Nd^{2}\rho^{4}),

where (a) is due to
\rho=\Omega(1)
, (b) hides the logarithmic factors.
Moreover, by
Lemma

D.2
, the network has width
O(d)=O(\rho^{2}d)
and depth
\tilde{O}(N)
.

Case II:
\rho\in(1/5\sqrt{d},1/3)
and
d<600\log N
.

In the second case, where
d<600\log N
and
(1/5\sqrt{d},1/3)
, the result also directly follows from the prior result by
Yu et al. 2024
.
In particular, we apply
Lemma

D.2
.
Let us denote
R:=\max_{i\in[N]}\left\lVert{\bm{x}}_{i}\right\rVert_{2}
and
\gamma:=(1-\rho)\epsilon_{\mathcal{D}}
.
Note that
R\geq\left\lVert{\bm{x}}_{i}\right\rVert_{\infty}
for all
i\in[N]
as
\left\lVert{\bm{x}}\right\rVert_{2}\geq\left\lVert{\bm{x}}\right\rVert_{\infty}
for all
{\bm{x}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
.
By
Lemma

D.2
, there exists
f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{d,P}
with
P=O(Nd^{2}(\log(\frac{d}{\gamma^{2}})+\log R))
parameters that
\rho
-robustly memorize
{\mathcal{D}}
.
The number of parameters can be further bounded as follows:

\displaystyle O(Nd^{2}(\log(\frac{d}{\gamma^{2}})+\log R))\overset{(a)}{=}O(N(\log N)^{2}\cdot(\log(\frac{d}{\gamma^{2}})+\log R))\overset{(b)}{=}\tilde{O}(N)\overset{(c)}{=}\tilde{O}(Nd^{2}\rho^{4}),

where (a) is due to
d\leq 600\log N
, (b) hides the logarithmic factors, and (c) is because
N\leq~625Nd^{2}\rho^{4}
for all
\rho\in\left(\frac{1}{5\sqrt{d}},\frac{1}{3}\right)
.
Moreover, by
Lemma

D.2
, the network has width
O(d)=O(\log N)=\tilde{O}(1)=\tilde{O}(\rho^{2}d)
and depth
\tilde{O}(N)
.

Case III:
\rho\in(1/5\sqrt{d},1/3)
and
N<600\log N\leq d
.

In the third case, where
N<600\log N\leq d
and
(1/5\sqrt{d},1/3)
, we first apply
Proposition

B.21
to
{\mathcal{D}}
to obtain 1-Lipschitz linear
\varphi:{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{N}
such that
{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime}:=\{(\varphi({\bm{x}}_{i}),y_{i})\}_{i\in[N]}
has
\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime}}=\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}}
.
This is possible as
d\geq N
.

We apply
Lemma

D.2
by
Yu et al. 2024
to
{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime}
.
Let us denote
R:=\max_{i\in[N]}\left\lVert\varphi({\bm{z}}_{i})\right\rVert_{2}
and
\gamma:=(1-\rho)\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime}}
.
Note that
R\geq\left\lVert\varphi({\bm{z}}_{i})\right\rVert_{\infty}
for all
i\in[N]
as
\left\lVert{\bm{z}}\right\rVert_{2}\geq\left\lVert{\bm{z}}\right\rVert_{\infty}
for all
{\bm{z}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{N}
.
By
Lemma

D.2
, there exists
f_{1}\in{\mathcal{F}}_{N,P}
with
P=O(N\cdot N^{2}(\log(\frac{N}{\gamma^{2}})+\log R))
parameters that
\rho
-robustly memorize
{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime}
.
f_{1}
has width
O(N)
and depth
\tilde{O}(N)
.

Let
f=f_{1}\circ\varphi
. This can be implemented by changing the first hidden layer matrix of
f_{1}
by composing
\varphi
. This is possible because
\varphi
is linear.

f
has at most
dN
additional parameters compared to
f_{1}
, and has same width and depth as
f_{1}
.
Since
f_{1}
is 1-Lipschitz and
\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime}}=\epsilon_{\mathcal{D}}
, every robustness ball of
{\mathcal{D}}
is mapped to the robustness ball of
{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime}
via
f_{1}
.
As
f_{1}

\rho
-robustly memorizes
{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime}
, the composed
f
satisfies the desired property.

The number of parameters can be further bounded as follows:

\displaystyle O(Nd+N\cdot N^{2}(\log(\frac{d}{\gamma^{2}})+\log R))\overset{(a)}{=}O(d\log N+(\log N)^{3}\cdot(\log(\frac{d}{\gamma^{2}})+\log R))\overset{(b)}{=}\tilde{O}(d),

where (a) is due to
N\leq 600\log N
, and (b) hides the logarithmic factors. The width of
f
is
O(N)=O(\log(N))=\tilde{O}(1)=\tilde{O}(\rho^{2}d)
. The depth of
f
is
\tilde{O}(N)
.

Case IV:
\rho\in(1/5\sqrt{d},1/3)
, and
N\geq d\geq 600\log N
.

In the fourth case, where
d\geq 600\log N
, we utilize the dimension reduction technique by
Proposition

B.19
.
We apply
Proposition

B.19
to
{\mathcal{D}}
with
m=\max\{\lceil 9d\rho^{2}\rceil,\lceil 600\log N\rceil,\lceil 10\log d\rceil\}
and
\alpha=1/5
.
Let us first check that the specified
m
satisfies the condition
24\alpha^{-2}\log N\leq m\leq d
for the proposition to be applied.

\alpha=1/5
and
m\geq 600\log N
ensure the first inequality
24\alpha^{-2}\log N\leq m
.
The second inequality
m\leq d
is decomposed into three parts.
Since
\rho\leq\frac{1}{3}
, we have
9d\rho^{2}\leq d
so that

\displaystyle\lceil 9d\rho^{2}\rceil\leq d.

(30)

Moreover,
600\log N\leq d
implies

\displaystyle\lceil 600\log N\rceil\leq d.

(31)

Additionally, as
N\geq 2
, we have
d\geq 600\log N\geq 600\log 2\geq 400
.
By
Lemma

B.22
, this implies
10\log d\leq d
and therefore

\displaystyle\lceil 10\log d\rceil\leq d.

(32)

Gathering
Equations

30
,
31
and
32
proves
m\leq d
.

By the
Proposition

B.19
, there exists 1-Lipschitz linear mapping
\phi:{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{m}
and
\beta>0
such that
{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime}:=\{(\phi({\bm{x}}_{i}),y_{i})\}_{i\in[N]}\in{\bm{D}}_{m,N,C}
satisfies

\displaystyle\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime}}\geq\frac{4}{5}\beta\epsilon_{\mathcal{D}}.

(33)

As
m\geq 10\log d
, the inequality
\beta\geq\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\frac{m}{d}}
is also satisfied by
Proposition

B.19
.
Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\beta\geq\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\frac{m}{d}}\overset{(a)}{\geq}\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\frac{\lceil 9d\rho^{2}\rceil}{d}}\geq\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\frac{9d\rho^{2}}{d}}=\frac{3}{2}\rho,

(34)

where (a) is by the definition of
m
.
Moreover, since
\phi
is 1-Lipschitz linear,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\phi({\bm{x}}_{i})\right\rVert_{2}=\left\lVert\phi({\bm{x}}_{i}-{\bm{0}})\right\rVert_{2}=\left\lVert\phi({\bm{x}}_{i})-\phi({\bm{0}})\right\rVert_{2}\leq\left\lVert{\bm{x}}_{i}-{\bm{0}}\right\rVert_{2}=\left\lVert{\bm{x}}_{i}\right\rVert_{2},

(35)

for all
i\in[N]
.
Hence, by letting
R:=\max_{i\in[N]}\{\left\lVert{\bm{x}}_{i}\right\rVert_{2}\}
, we have
\left\lVert\phi({\bm{x}}_{i})\right\rVert_{2}\leq R
for all
i\in[N]
.

Now, we set the first layer hidden matrix as the matrix
{\bm{W}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{m\times d}
corresponding to
\phi
under the standard basis of
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
and
{\mathbb{R}}^{m}
.
Moreover, set the first hidden layer bias as
{\bm{b}}:=2R{\bm{1}}=2R(1,1,\cdots,1)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{m}
.
Then, we have

\displaystyle{\bm{W}}{\bm{x}}+{\bm{b}}\geq{\bm{0}},

(36)

for all
{\bm{x}}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{2}({\bm{x}}_{i},\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}})
for all
i\in[N]
, where the comparison between two vectors are element-wise.
This is because for all
i\in[N],j\in[m]
and
{\bm{x}}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{2}({\bm{x}},\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}})
, we have

\displaystyle({\bm{W}}{\bm{x}}+{\bm{b}})_{j}=({\bm{W}}{\bm{x}})_{j}+2R\geq 2R-\left\lVert{\bm{W}}{\bm{x}}\right\rVert_{2}\overset{(a)}{\geq}2R-\left\lVert{\bm{x}}\right\rVert_{2}\overset{(b)}{\geq}2R-(R+\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}})\overset{(c)}{\geq}0,

where (a) is by
Equation

35
, (b) is by the triangle inequality, and (c) is due to
R>\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}}
.

We construct the first layer of the neural network as
f_{1}({\bm{x}}):=\sigma({\bm{W}}{\bm{x}}+{\bm{b}})
which includes the activation
\sigma
.
Then, by above properties,
{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}:=\{(f_{1}({\bm{x}}_{i}),y_{i})\}_{i\in[N]}
satisfies

\displaystyle\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}}\geq\frac{6}{5}\rho\epsilon_{\mathcal{D}}.

(37)

This is because for
i\neq j
with
y_{i}\neq y_{j}
we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert f_{1}({\bm{x}}_{i})-f_{1}({\bm{x}}_{j})\right\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\sigma({\bm{W}}{\bm{x}}_{i}+{\bm{b}})-\sigma({\bm{W}}{\bm{x}}_{j}+{\bm{b}})\right\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{=}\left\lVert({\bm{W}}{\bm{x}}_{i}+{\bm{b}})-({\bm{W}}{\bm{x}}_{j}+{\bm{b}})\right\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\phi({\bm{x}}_{i})-\phi({\bm{x}}_{k})\right\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\geq}2\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\overset{(c)}{\geq}2\times\frac{4}{5}\beta\epsilon_{\mathcal{D}}

\displaystyle\overset{(d)}{\geq}2\times\frac{4}{5}\times\frac{3}{2}\rho\epsilon_{\mathcal{D}}

\displaystyle=\frac{12}{5}\rho\epsilon_{\mathcal{D}},

where (a) is by
Equation

36
, (b) is by the definition of the
\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime}}
, (c) is by
Equation

33
, and (d) is by
Equation

34
.
By
Lemma

D.2
applied to
{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}\in{\bm{D}}_{m,N,C}
, there exists
f_{2}\in{\mathcal{F}}_{m,P}
with
P=O(Nm^{2}(\log(\frac{m}{(\gamma^{\prime\prime})^{2}})+\log R^{\prime\prime}))
number of parameters that
\frac{5}{6}
-robustly memorize
{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}
, where

\displaystyle\gamma^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle:=(1-\frac{5}{6})\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}}\overset{(a)}{\geq}\frac{1}{6}\times\frac{12}{5}\rho\epsilon_{\mathcal{D}}=\frac{2}{5}\rho\epsilon_{\mathcal{D}},

\displaystyle R^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle:=\max_{i\in[N]}\left\lVert f_{1}({\bm{x}}_{i})\right\rVert_{2}=\max_{i\in[N]}\left\lVert\sigma({\bm{W}}{\bm{x}}_{i}+{\bm{b}})\right\rVert_{2}=\max_{i\in[N]}\left\lVert{\bm{W}}{\bm{x}}_{i}+{\bm{b}}\right\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{i\in[N]}\left\lVert{\bm{W}}{\bm{x}}_{i}\right\rVert_{2}+\left\lVert{\bm{b}}\right\rVert_{2}\leq 3R.

Here (a) is by
Equation

37
.
Moreover
f_{2}
has width
O(m)
and depth
\tilde{O}(N)
by
Lemma

D.2
.

Now, we claim that
f:=f_{2}\circ f_{1}

\rho
-robustly memorize
{\mathcal{D}}
.
For any
i\in[N]
, take
{\bm{x}}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{2}({\bm{x}}_{i},\rho\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}})
.
Then, by
Equation

36
, we have
f_{1}({\bm{x}})={\bm{W}}{\bm{x}}+{\bm{b}}
and
f_{1}({\bm{x}}_{i})={\bm{W}}{\bm{x}}_{i}+{\bm{b}}
so that

\displaystyle\left\lVert f_{1}({\bm{x}})-f_{1}({\bm{x}}_{i})\right\rVert_{2}=\left\lVert{\bm{W}}{\bm{x}}-{\bm{W}}{\bm{x}}_{i}\right\rVert_{2}\leq\left\lVert{\bm{x}}-{\bm{x}}_{i}\right\rVert_{2}\leq\rho\epsilon_{\mathcal{D}}.

(38)

Moreover, combining
Equations

37
and
38
results
\left\lVert f_{1}({\bm{x}})-f_{1}({\bm{x}}_{i})\right\rVert_{2}\leq\frac{5}{6}\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}}
.
Since
f_{2}

\frac{5}{6}
-robustly memorize
{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}
, we have

\displaystyle f({\bm{x}})=f_{2}(f_{1}({\bm{x}}))=f_{2}(f_{1}({\bm{x}}_{i}))=y_{i}.

In particular,
f({\bm{x}})=y_{i}
for any
{\bm{x}}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{2}({\bm{x}}_{i},\rho\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}})
, concluding that
f
is a
\rho
-robust memorizer
{\mathcal{D}}
.
Regarding the number of parameters to construct
f
, notice that
f_{1}
consists of
(d+1)m=\tilde{O}(d^{2}\rho^{2})
parameters as
m=\tilde{O}(d\rho^{2})
.
f_{2}
consists of
\tilde{O}(Nm^{2})=\tilde{O}(Nd^{2}\rho^{4})
parameters.
Since the case IV assumes
N\geq d
and large
\rho
regime deals with
\rho\geq\frac{1}{5\sqrt{d}}
, we have

\displaystyle d^{2}\rho^{2}\leq Nd\rho^{2}\leq 25Nd^{2}\rho^{4}

Therefore,
f
in total consists of
\tilde{O}(d^{2}\rho^{2}+Nd^{2}\rho^{4})=\tilde{O}(Nd^{2}\rho^{4})
number of parameters.
Moreover, since
f
has the same width as
f_{2}
and depth one larger than the depth of
f_{2}
, it follows that
f
has width
O(m)=\tilde{O}(\rho^{2}d)
and depth
\tilde{O}(N)
.
This proves the theorem for the fourth case.

Case V:
\rho\in(1/5\sqrt{d},1/3)
, and
d>N\geq 600\log N
.

The last case combines the two techniques used in Cases III and IV.
We first apply
Proposition

B.21
to
{\mathcal{D}}
to obtain 1-Lipschitz linear
