\displaystyle\eta^{\prime}\frac{\operatorname{Vol}({\mathcal{B}}_{2}({\bm{x}}_{i},\rho^{\prime}\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}},2}))}{\operatorname{Vol}({\mathcal{B}}_{p}({\bm{x}}_{i},\rho\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}},p}))}

\displaystyle\overset{(c)}{=}

\displaystyle\eta,

where (a) holds by
Equation

65
, (b) holds by
Equation

66
, and (c) holds by the definition of
\eta^{\prime}
.

Thus, for all
({\bm{x}}_{i},y_{i})\in{\mathcal{D}}
, it holds:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{{\bm{x}}^{\prime}\sim\text{Unif}({\mathcal{B}}_{p}({\bm{x}}_{i},\rho\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}},p}))}[f({\bm{x}}^{\prime})\neq y_{i}]<\eta.

In other words,
f

\rho
-robustly memorizes
{\mathcal{D}}
under
\ell_{p}
-norm with error at most
\eta
and
\tilde{O}(Nd^{\frac{1}{4}}\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}\gamma_{p}(d)^{\frac{1}{2}})
parameters.

∎

Theorem C.14
.

Let
\rho\in\Big(\frac{1}{5\sqrt{d}\gamma_{p}(d)},\frac{1}{\gamma_{p}(d)}\Big)
and
p\in[1,\infty]
.
For any dataset
{\mathcal{D}}\in{\bm{D}}_{d,N,C}
, there exists
f\in\mathcal{F}_{d,P}
with
P=\tilde{O}(Nd^{2}\rho^{4}\gamma_{p}(d)^{4})
that
\rho
-robustly memorizes
{\mathcal{D}}
under
\ell_{p}
-norm.

Proof.

Let
\rho^{\prime}=\gamma_{p}(d)\rho
. Then, we have
\rho^{\prime}\in\Big(\frac{1}{5\sqrt{d}},1\Big)
from the condition of
\rho
.
By Theorem
4.2(iii)
, there exists
f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{d,P}
with
P=\tilde{O}(Nd^{2}\rho^{\prime 4})=\tilde{O}(Nd^{2}\rho^{4}{\gamma_{p}(d)}^{4})
that
\rho^{\prime}
-robustly memorizes
{\mathcal{D}}
under
\ell_{2}
-norm. In other words, it holds
f({\bm{x}}^{\prime})=y_{i}
, for all
({\bm{x}}_{i},y_{i})\in{\mathcal{D}}
and
{\bm{x}}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{2}({\bm{x}}_{i},\rho^{\prime}\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}},2})
.

We consider two cases depending on whether
p\geq 2
or
p<2
, which affect the direction of inclusion between
\ell_{p}
and
\ell_{2}
balls.

Case I :
p\geq 2
.

In this case, we have:

{\mathcal{B}}_{p}({\bm{x}}_{i},\rho\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}},p})\overset{(a)}{\subseteq}{\mathcal{B}}_{p}({\bm{x}}_{i},\rho\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}},2})\overset{(b)}{\subseteq}{\mathcal{B}}_{2}({\bm{x}}_{i},\gamma_{p}(d)\rho\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}},2})={\mathcal{B}}_{2}({\bm{x}}_{i},\rho^{\prime}\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}},2}),

where (a) holds by
Equation

61
and (b) holds by
Lemma

C.4
applying
p=2
and
q=p
.

Thus, for all
({\bm{x}}_{i},y_{i})\in{\mathcal{D}}
and
{\bm{x}}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{p}({\bm{x}}_{i},\rho\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}},p})
, it also holds
f({\bm{x}}^{\prime})=y_{i}
. In other words,
f

\rho
-robustly memorizes
{\mathcal{D}}
under
\ell_{p}
-norm with
\tilde{O}(Nd^{2}\rho^{4}{\gamma_{p}(d)}^{4})
parameters.

Case II :
p<2
.

In this case, we have:

{\mathcal{B}}_{p}({\bm{x}}_{i},\rho\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}},p})\overset{(a)}{\subseteq}{\mathcal{B}}_{p}({\bm{x}}_{i},\gamma_{p}(d)\rho\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}},2})\overset{(b)}{\subseteq}{\mathcal{B}}_{2}({\bm{x}}_{i},\gamma_{p}(d)\rho\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}},2})={\mathcal{B}}_{2}({\bm{x}}_{i},\rho^{\prime}\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}},2}),

where (a) holds by
Equation

62
and (b) holds by
Lemma

C.4
applying
p=p
and
q=2
.

Thus, for all
({\bm{x}}_{i},y_{i})\in{\mathcal{D}}
and
{\bm{x}}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{p}({\bm{x}}_{i},\rho\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}},p})
, it also holds
f({\bm{x}}^{\prime})=y_{i}
. In other words,
f

\rho
-robustly memorizes
{\mathcal{D}}
under
\ell_{p}
-norm with
\tilde{O}(Nd^{2}\rho^{4}{\gamma_{p}(d)}^{4})
parameters.

∎

D.2
Parameter Complexity of the Construction by
Yu et al. 2024

We now analyze the number of parameters of the network construction proposed by
Yu et al. 2024
, which provides the upper bound not depending on
\rho
, but still applies to all
\rho\in(0,1)
.

Lemma D.1

(Theorem B.6,
Yu et al. 2024
)
.

Let
p\in{\mathbb{N}}
.
For any dataset
{\mathcal{D}}=\{({\bm{x}}_{i},y_{i})\}_{i\in[N]}\in{\bm{D}}_{d,N,C}
, let
R>1
by any real value with
\left\lVert{\bm{x}}_{i}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq R
for all
i\in[N]
.
For
\rho\in(0,1)
, define
\gamma:=(1-\rho)\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}},p}>0
.
Then, there exists a network with width
O(d)
, and depth
O(Np(\log(\frac{d}{\gamma^{p}})+p\log R+\log p))
that
\rho
-robustly memorize
{\mathcal{D}}
under
\ell_{p}
-norm.

We note that in the
Yu et al. 2024
uses the notation
\lambda_{\mathcal{D}}^{p}/2
for
\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}},p}
, and the radius
\lambda_{\mathcal{D}}^{p}/2-\gamma
in the original statement corresponds to the value
\mu:=\rho\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}},p}
in our notation.
We count parameters in their construction in the following lemma, specifically in the case
p=2
.
Although the original statement of
Yu et al. 2024
includes a parameter count, they consider a different parameter counting strategy–by counting only the number of nonzero parameters.
We therefore count the number of all parameters following
Equation

3
in the subsequent lemma.
Note that results and comparison under nonzero parameter counts are provided in
Appendix

E
.

Lemma D.2
.

For any
\mathcal{D}\in{\bm{D}}_{d,N,C}
and
\rho\in(0,1)
, define
\gamma:=(1-\rho)\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}}>0
and
R>1
with
\left\lVert{\bm{x}}_{i}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq R
for all
i\in[N]
.
Then, there exists a neural network
f
such that
\rho
-robustly memorizes
\mathcal{D}
using at most
O(Nd^{2}(\log(\frac{d}{\gamma^{2}})+\log R))
parameters. Moreover, the network has width
O(d)
and depth
\tilde{O}(N)
.

Proof.

By applying
Lemma

D.1
with
p=2
, we obtain a neural network
f
that
\rho
-robustly memorizes
\mathcal{D}
with width
O(d)
, and depth
L=O(N(\log(\frac{d}{\gamma^{2}}+\log R)))
.
In their construction,
d_{l}=\Theta(d)
through all
l
, as the input
x
propagates over the layers using a width
d
.
We count all parameters as defined in
Equation

3
, so we can upper bound the number of parameters used for the construction of
f
as follows:

\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{L}(d_{l-1}+1)\cdot d_{l}

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=1}^{L}\Theta(d)\cdot\Theta(d)

\displaystyle=\Theta(N(\log(\frac{d}{\gamma^{2}})+\log R))\cdot\Theta(d^{2})

\displaystyle=\Theta(Nd^{2}(\log(\frac{d}{\gamma^{2}})+\log R)).

∎

D.3
Parameter Complexity of the Construction by
Egosi et al. 2025

We observe that although
Egosi et al. 2025
do not explicitly quantify the total number of parameters in their construction, it implicitly yields a network with
O(Nd^{3}\rho^{6})
parameters. Specifically, we can establish the following:

For any
\mathcal{D}\in{\bm{D}}_{d,N,C}
and
\rho\in(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}},1)
, there exists a neural network
f
that
\rho
-robustly memorizes
\mathcal{D}
using
\tilde{O}(Nd^{3}\rho^{6})
parameters.

This result follows from the network constructed in Theorem 4.4 of
Egosi et al. 2025
.
The proof of Theorem 4.4 proceeds under the assumption that for
7\leq k\leq d+5
, and
\rho\leq\frac{1}{4\sqrt{e}}\sqrt{\frac{k-6}{d}}N^{-\frac{2}{k-6}}
.
Given this range, Theorem 4.2 of
Egosi et al. 2025
is applied to construct a robust memorizer of the projected data from
\mathbb{R}^{d}
to
\mathbb{R}^{k}
.
Figures 4 and 5 in their paper illustrate this construction.
In this construction, the projected point propagates through the network
\Theta(Nk)
times.
The width of the network scales with
k
, while the other component, that is not propagating the point remains constant in width.
Thus, the number of parameters used for the construction is given by:

\sum_{l=1}^{L}(d_{l-1}+1)\cdot d_{l}=\sum_{l=1}^{\Theta(Nk)}\Theta(k^{2})=\Theta(Nk\cdot k^{2})=\Theta(Nk^{3}).

To translate this to a bound in terms of
\rho
, we analyze the relationship between
\rho
and
k
.
For
k\geq 4\log N+6
, we verify the following inequality:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4\sqrt{e}}\sqrt{\frac{k-6}{d}}N^{-\frac{2}{k-6}}\geq\frac{1}{4\sqrt{e}}\sqrt{\frac{k-6}{d}}N^{-\frac{1}{2\log N}}\overset{(a)}{=}\frac{1}{4e}\sqrt{\frac{k-6}{d}}

where (a) holds by
N=e^{\log N}
.
Therefore, for
\rho=\frac{1}{4e}\sqrt{\frac{k-6}{d}}
, the network
\rho
-robustly memorizes
{\mathcal{D}}
with
\Theta(Nk^{3})
parameters. From the relationship between
\rho
and
k
, solving for
k
in terms of
\rho
yields
k=\Theta(d\rho^{2})
.
Since the minimum value of
k
under the assumption is
7
, the minimum achievable
\rho
is
\frac{1}{4e}\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}
.

Thus, for
\rho>\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}
, the construction yields a network that
\rho
-robustly memorizes
{\mathcal{D}}
with
\Theta(Nk^{3})=\Theta(Nd^{3}\rho^{6})
parameters, as desired.

Appendix E
Nonzero Parameter Counts

While our main parameter counting method follows the approach of counting all parameters, including zeros, as defined in
Equation

3
, some prior works on memorization and robust memorization adopt a different parameter counting strategy—counting only the nonzero parameters.
We emphasize that counting all parameters, including zeros, better aligns with how the matrices are stored in practice.
Nevertheless, we also present how our results extend to the case of counting only nonzero parameters, offering an alternative perspective for interpreting our findings and comparing them with prior work.

In contrast to
Equation

4
, let us define the set of neural networks with input dimension
d
and at most
P
nonzero parameters by

\displaystyle\bar{{\mathcal{F}}}_{d,P}=\left\{f:{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}\mid f\textrm{ is a neural network with at most }P\textrm{ nonzero parameters}\right\}.

(67)

E.1
Nonzero Parameter Counts: An illustration.

We provide the corresponding illustration of
Figure

1
under only nonzero parameter counting in
Figure

8
, combining
Theorem

E.1
and
Theorem

E.2
.

Figure 8
:
Summary of parameter bounds, counting only nonzero parameters on a log-log scale when
d=\Theta(\sqrt{N})
. We omit constant factors in both axes.
Solid blue and red curves show the sufficient (
Theorem

E.2
) and necessary (
Theorem

E.1
) numbers of parameters, respectively; the solid black curve is the best prior bound.
Light-blue shading highlights our improvement in the upper bound, and light-red shading highlights our improvement in the lower bound. The cross-hatched area marks the remaining gap.

\frac{1}{N\sqrt{d}}
\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}
\frac{1}{\sqrt[4]{d}}

\sqrt{N}

E.2
Nonzero Parameter Counts: Lower Bounds

The lower bound in
Theorem

3.1
that counts all parameters consists of two terms: one based on the network width and another based on the VC-dimension.
Although the lower bound by VC-dimension remains valid even when counting only nonzero parameters, the lower bound on the first hidden layer width can be translated into a lower bound on parameters only if we also include zero-valued parameters in the parameter counting convention. As a result, we obtain the following lower bound consisting of only the lower bound from the VC-dimension.

Theorem E.1
.

Let
\rho\in(0,1)
. Suppose for any
{\mathcal{D}}\in{\bm{D}}_{d,N,2}
, there exists a neural network
f\in\bar{{\mathcal{F}}}_{d,P}
that can
\rho
-robustly memorize
{\mathcal{D}}
.
Then, the number of parameters
P
must satisfy

\displaystyle P=\Omega\left(\min\left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}},\sqrt{d}\right\}\sqrt{N}\right).

The main reason why the VC-dimension lower bound remains valid even for the nonzero parameter count is because the key relation
\mathrm{VC\text{-}dim}(\bar{{\mathcal{F}}}_{d,P})=O(P^{2})

[
Goldberg and Jerrum 1995
]
holds even for the
\bar{{\mathcal{F}}}_{d,P}
instead of
{\mathcal{F}}_{d,P}
.
Below, we provide an explicit proof of the
Theorem

E.1
.

Proof.

Since
{\mathcal{F}}_{d,P}\subseteq\bar{{\mathcal{F}}}_{d,P}
, we have
\mathrm{VC\text{-}dim}({\mathcal{F}}_{d,P})\leq\mathrm{VC\text{-}dim}(\bar{{\mathcal{F}}}_{d,P})
. In particular, by
Equation

7
, we have for
\rho\in\left(0,\sqrt{1-\frac{1}{d}}\right]
that

\displaystyle\mathrm{VC\text{-}dim}(\bar{{\mathcal{F}}}_{d,P})\geq\mathrm{VC\text{-}dim}({\mathcal{F}}_{d,P})=\Omega\left(\frac{N}{1-\rho^{2}}\right).

By
[
Goldberg and Jerrum 1995
]
, we have
\mathrm{VC\text{-}dim}(\bar{{\mathcal{F}}}_{d,P})=O(P^{2})
.
Combining the two relations proves that for
\rho\in\left(0,\sqrt{1-\frac{1}{d}}\right]
,

\displaystyle P=\Omega\left(\sqrt{\frac{N}{1-\rho^{2}}}\right).

Since
\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}}\leq\sqrt{d}
for
\rho\in\left(0,\sqrt{1-\frac{1}{d}}\right]
, the following relation holds:

\displaystyle\min\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}},\sqrt{d}\}\sqrt{N}=\sqrt{\frac{N}{1-\rho^{2}}}.

For
\rho\in\left(\sqrt{1-\frac{1}{d}},1\right)
, the lower bound
P=\Omega(\sqrt{Nd})
obtained by the case
\rho=\sqrt{1-\frac{1}{d}}
also can be applied.
Since
\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}}>\sqrt{d}
for
\rho\in\left(\sqrt{1-\frac{1}{d}},1\right)
, the following relation holds:

\displaystyle\min\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}},\sqrt{d}\}\sqrt{N}=\sqrt{Nd}.

As a result, applying
P=\Omega\left(\sqrt{\frac{N}{1-\rho^{2}}}\right)
for
\rho\in\left(0,\sqrt{1-\frac{1}{d}}\right]
and
\Omega(\sqrt{Nd})
for
\rho\in\left(\sqrt{1-\frac{1}{d}},1\right)
results in

\displaystyle P=\Omega\left(\min\left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}},\sqrt{d}\right\}\sqrt{N}\right)

∎

E.3
Nonzero Parameter Counts: Upper Bounds

While upper bounds on parameter counts of all parameters in
Theorem

4.2
are naturally an upper bound for parameter counts of nonzero parameters, we provide a tighter upper bound regarding the nonzero parameters.

Theorem E.2
.

For any dataset
{\mathcal{D}}\in{\bm{D}}_{d,N,C}
and
\eta\in(0,1)
, the following statements hold:

(i)

If
\rho\in\Big(0,\frac{1}{{5}N\sqrt{d}}\Big]
, there exists
f\in\bar{{\mathcal{F}}}_{d,P}
with
P=\tilde{O}(\sqrt{N}+d)
that
\rho
-robustly memorizes
{\mathcal{D}}
.

(ii)

If
\rho\in\Big(\frac{1}{{5}N\sqrt{d}},\frac{1}{5\sqrt{d}}\Big]
, there exists
f\in\bar{{\mathcal{F}}}_{d,P}
with
P=\tilde{O}(Nd^{\frac{1}{4}}\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}+d)
that
\rho
-robustly memorizes
{\mathcal{D}}
with error at most
\eta
.

(iii)

If
\rho\in\Big(\frac{1}{5\sqrt{d}},1\Big)
, there exists
f\in\bar{{\mathcal{F}}}_{d,P}
with
P=\tilde{O}(Nd\rho^{2}+d)
that
\rho
-robustly memorizes
{\mathcal{D}}
.

In comparison to the total parameter count as in
Theorem

4.2
, only Theorem
E.2(iii)
have a modified rate from
P=\tilde{O}(Nd^{2}\rho^{4})
to
P=\tilde{O}(Nd\rho^{2})
.
Below, we provide an explicit proof of
Theorem

E.2
.
The
d
term in the parameter bounds of all three cases comes from the upper bound on the parameters of the first hidden layer.

Proof.

Upper bounds on all parameter counts are natural upper bounds on the nonzero parameter counts.
Since Theorem
E.2(i)
and Theorem
E.2(ii)
claims the same rate as Theorem
4.2(i)
and Theorem
4.2(ii)
respectively, they trivially follows from
Theorem

4.2
.
Another way of speaking,
{\mathcal{F}}_{d,P}\subseteq\bar{{\mathcal{F}}}_{d,P}
and the first two cases directly follow from
Theorem

4.2
.

Now let us prove Theorem
E.2(iii)
.
Here, we mainly follow the proof of
Theorem

B.14
, where instead of counting every parameter using
Lemma

D.2
, we count only the nonzero parameters using
Lemma

E.3
.
We divide the cases into five, following
Theorem

B.14
as in
Figure

6
.

Let
{\mathcal{D}}=\{({\bm{x}}_{i},y_{i})\}_{i\in[N]}\in{\bm{D}}_{d,N,C}
be given.
We divide the proof into five cases, the first case under
\rho\in[1/3,1)
, the second case under
\rho\in(1/5\sqrt{d},1/3)
and
d<600\log N
, the third case under
\rho\in(1/5\sqrt{d},1/3)
and
N<600\log N\leq d
, the fourth case under
\rho\in(1/5\sqrt{d},1/3)
,
N\geq d\geq 600\log N
, and finally the fifth case under
\rho\in(1/5\sqrt{d},1/3)
and
d>N\geq 600\log N
.
To check that these cases cover all the cases, refer to
Figure

6
.

Case I:
\rho\in[1/3,1)
.

Let us denote
R:=\max_{i\in[N]}\left\lVert{\bm{x}}_{i}\right\rVert_{2}
and
\gamma:=(1-\rho)\epsilon_{\mathcal{D}}
.
Note that
R\geq\left\lVert{\bm{x}}_{i}\right\rVert_{\infty}
for all
i\in[N]
as
\left\lVert{\bm{x}}\right\rVert_{2}\geq\left\lVert{\bm{x}}\right\rVert_{\infty}
for all
{\bm{x}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
.
By applying
Lemma

E.3
, there exists
f\in\bar{{\mathcal{F}}}_{d,P}
with
P=O(Nd(\log(\frac{d}{\gamma^{2}})+\log R))

nonzero
parameters that
\rho
-robustly memorize
{\mathcal{D}}
.
The number of
nonzero
parameters can be further bounded as follows:

\displaystyle O(Nd(\log(\frac{d}{\gamma^{2}})+\log R))\overset{(a)}{=}O(Nd\rho^{2}\cdot(\log(\frac{d}{\gamma^{2}})+\log R))\overset{(b)}{=}\tilde{O}(Nd\rho^{2}),

where (a) is due to
\rho=\Omega(1)
, (b) hides the logarithmic factors.

Case II:
\rho\in(1/5\sqrt{d},1/3)
and
d<600\log N
.

Let us denote
R:=\max_{i\in[N]}\left\lVert{\bm{x}}_{i}\right\rVert_{2}
and
\gamma:=(1-\rho)\epsilon_{\mathcal{D}}
.
Note that
R\geq\left\lVert{\bm{x}}_{i}\right\rVert_{\infty}
for all
i\in[N]
as
\left\lVert{\bm{x}}\right\rVert_{2}\geq\left\lVert{\bm{x}}\right\rVert_{\infty}
for all
{\bm{x}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
.
By
Lemma

E.3
, there exists
f\in\bar{{\mathcal{F}}}_{d,P}
with
P=O(Nd(\log(\frac{d}{\gamma^{2}})+\log R))

nonzero
parameters that
\rho
-robustly memorize
{\mathcal{D}}
.
The number of
nonzero
parameters can be further bounded as follows:

\displaystyle O(Nd(\log(\frac{d}{\gamma^{2}})+\log R))\overset{(a)}{=}O(N(\log N)\cdot(\log(\frac{d}{\gamma^{2}})+\log R))\overset{(b)}{=}\tilde{O}(N)\overset{(c)}{=}\tilde{O}(Nd\rho^{2}),

where (a) is due to
d\leq 600\log N
, (b) hides the logarithmic factors, and (c) is because
N\leq~25Nd\rho^{2}
for all
\rho\in\left(\frac{1}{5\sqrt{d}},\frac{1}{3}\right)
.

Case III:
\rho\in(1/5\sqrt{d},1/3)
and
N<600\log N\leq d
.

We first apply
Proposition

B.21
to
{\mathcal{D}}
to obtain 1-Lipschitz linear
\varphi:{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{N}
such that
{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime}:=\{(\varphi({\bm{x}}_{i}),y_{i})\}_{i\in[N]}
has
\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime}}=\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}}
.
This is possible as
d\geq N
.

Take
{\bm{b}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{N}
such that
\varphi({\bm{x}})-{\bm{b}}\geq{\bm{0}}
for all
{\bm{x}}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{2}(\varphi({\bm{x}}_{i}),\rho\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime}})
, ensuring that
{\sigma}
does not affect the output of the first hidden layer.
Let
{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}=\{(\varphi({\bm{x}}_{i})-{\bm{b}},y_{i})\}_{i\in[N]}
.
Then,
\epsilon_{\mathcal{D}}=\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime}}=\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}}
.
For simplicity of the notation, let us denote
{\bm{z}}_{i}:=\varphi({\bm{x}}_{i})-{\bm{b}}
.
Moreover, the first hidden layer is defined as
f_{1}({\bm{x}})=\varphi({\bm{x}})-{\bm{b}}
.

We apply
Lemma

E.3
to
{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}
.
Let us denote
R:=\max_{i\in[N]}\left\lVert\varphi({\bm{z}}_{i})\right\rVert_{2}
and
\gamma:=(1-\rho)\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}}
.
Note that
R\geq\left\lVert{\bm{z}}_{i}\right\rVert_{\infty}
for all
i\in[N]
as
\left\lVert{\bm{z}}\right\rVert_{2}\geq\left\lVert{\bm{z}}\right\rVert_{\infty}
for all
{\bm{z}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{N}
.
By
Lemma

E.3
, there exists
f_{2}\in\bar{{\mathcal{F}}}_{N,P}
with
P=O(N\cdot N(\log(\frac{N}{\gamma^{2}})+\log R))

nonzero
parameters that
\rho
-robustly memorize
{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}
.

Let
f=f_{2}\circ{\sigma}\circ f_{1}
.
Since
f_{1}
is 1-Lipschitz and
\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}}=\epsilon_{\mathcal{D}}
, every robustness ball of
{\mathcal{D}}
is mapped to the robustness ball of
{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}
via
f_{1}
.
Since the
{\sigma}
does not affect the first hidden layer output of the robustness ball, and
f_{2}

\rho
-robustly memorizes
{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}
, the composed
f
satisfies the desired property

The number of
nonzero
parameters can be further bounded as follows:

\displaystyle O(Nd+N\cdot N(\log(\frac{d}{\gamma^{2}})+\log R))\overset{(a)}{=}O(d\log N+(\log N)^{2}\cdot(\log(\frac{d}{\gamma^{2}})+\log R))\overset{(b)}{=}\tilde{O}(d),

where (a) is due to
N\leq 600\log N
, and (b) hides the logarithmic factors.

Case IV:
\rho\in(1/5\sqrt{d},1/3)
, and
N\geq d\geq 600\log N
.

We utilize the dimension reduction technique by
Proposition

B.19
.
We apply
Proposition

B.19
to
{\mathcal{D}}
with
m=\max\{\lceil 9d\rho^{2}\rceil,\lceil 600\log N\rceil,\lceil 10\log d\rceil\}
and
\alpha=1/5
.
Let us first check that the specified
m
satisfies the condition
24\alpha^{-2}\log N\leq m\leq d
for the proposition to be applied.

\alpha=1/5
and
m\geq 600\log N
ensure the first inequality
24\alpha^{-2}\log N\leq m
.
The second inequality
m\leq d
is decomposed into three parts.
Since
\rho\leq\frac{1}{3}
, we have
9d\rho^{2}\leq d
so that

\displaystyle\lceil 9d\rho^{2}\rceil\leq d.

(68)

Moreover,
600\log N\leq d
implies

\displaystyle\lceil 600\log N\rceil\leq d.

(69)

Additionally, as
N\geq 2
, we have
d\geq 600\log N\geq 600\log 2\geq 400
.
By
Lemma

B.22
, this implies
10\log d\leq d
and therefore

\displaystyle\lceil 10\log d\rceil\leq d.

(70)

Gathering
Equations

68
,
69
and
70
proves
m\leq d
.

By the
Proposition

B.19
, there exists 1-Lipchitz linear mapping
\phi:{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{m}
and
\beta>0
such that
{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime}:=\{(\phi({\bm{x}}_{i}),y_{i})\}_{i\in[N]}\in{\bm{D}}_{m,N,C}
satisfies

\displaystyle\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime}}\geq\frac{4}{5}\beta\epsilon_{\mathcal{D}}.

(71)

As
m\geq 10\log d
, the inequality
\beta\geq\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\frac{m}{d}}
is also satisfied by
Proposition

B.19
.
Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\beta\geq\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\frac{m}{d}}\overset{(a)}{\geq}\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\frac{\lceil 9d\rho^{2}\rceil}{d}}\geq\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\frac{9d\rho^{2}}{d}}=\frac{3}{2}\rho,

(72)

where (a) is by the definition of
m
.
Moreover, since
\phi
is 1-Lipchitz linear,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\phi({\bm{x}}_{i})\right\rVert_{2}=\left\lVert\phi({\bm{x}}_{i}-{\bm{0}})\right\rVert_{2}=\left\lVert\phi({\bm{x}}_{i})-\phi({\bm{0}})\right\rVert_{2}\leq\left\lVert{\bm{x}}_{i}-{\bm{0}}\right\rVert_{2}=\left\lVert{\bm{x}}_{i}\right\rVert_{2},

(73)

for all
i\in[N]
.
Hence, by letting
R:=\max_{i\in[N]}\{\left\lVert{\bm{x}}_{i}\right\rVert_{2}\}
, we have
\left\lVert\phi({\bm{x}}_{i})\right\rVert_{2}\leq R
for all
i\in[N]
.

Now, we set the first layer hidden matrix as the matrix
{\bm{W}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{m\times d}
corresponding to
\phi
under the standard basis of
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
and
{\mathbb{R}}^{m}
.
Moreover, set the first hidden layer bias as
{\bm{b}}:=2R{\bm{1}}=2R(1,1,\cdots,1)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{m}
.
Then, we have

\displaystyle{\bm{W}}{\bm{x}}+{\bm{b}}\geq{\bm{0}},

(74)

for all
{\bm{x}}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{2}({\bm{x}}_{i},\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}})
for all
i\in[N]
, where the comparison between two vectors are element-wise.
This is because for all
i\in[N],j\in[m]
and
{\bm{x}}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{2}({\bm{x}},\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}})
, we have

\displaystyle({\bm{W}}{\bm{x}}+{\bm{b}})_{j}=({\bm{W}}{\bm{x}})_{j}+2R\geq 2R-\left\lVert{\bm{W}}{\bm{x}}\right\rVert_{2}\overset{(a)}{\geq}2R-\left\lVert{\bm{x}}\right\rVert_{2}\overset{(b)}{\geq}2R-(R+\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}})\overset{(c)}{\geq}0,

where (a) is by
Equation

73
, (b) is by the triangle inequality, and (c) is due to
R>\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}}
.

We construct the first layer of the neural network as
f_{1}({\bm{x}}):=\sigma({\bm{W}}{\bm{x}}+{\bm{b}})
which includes the activation
\sigma
.
Then, by above properties,
{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}:=\{(f_{1}({\bm{x}}_{i}),y_{i})\}_{i\in[N]}
satisfies

\displaystyle\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}}\geq\frac{6}{5}\rho\epsilon_{\mathcal{D}}.

(75)

This is because for
i\neq j
with
y_{i}\neq y_{j}
we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert f_{1}({\bm{x}}_{i})-f_{1}({\bm{x}}_{j})\right\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\sigma({\bm{W}}{\bm{x}}_{i}+{\bm{b}})-\sigma({\bm{W}}{\bm{x}}_{j}+{\bm{b}})\right\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{=}\left\lVert({\bm{W}}{\bm{x}}_{i}+{\bm{b}})-({\bm{W}}{\bm{x}}_{j}+{\bm{b}})\right\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\phi({\bm{x}}_{i})-\phi({\bm{x}}_{j})\right\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\geq}2\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\overset{(c)}{\geq}2\times\frac{4}{5}\beta\epsilon_{\mathcal{D}}

\displaystyle\overset{(d)}{\geq}2\times\frac{4}{5}\times\frac{3}{2}\rho\epsilon_{\mathcal{D}}

\displaystyle=\frac{12}{5}\rho\epsilon_{\mathcal{D}},

where (a) is by
Equation

74
, (b) is by the definition of the
\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime}}
, (c) is by
Equation

71
, and (d) is by
Equation

72
.
By
Lemma

E.3
applied to
{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}\in{\bm{D}}_{m,N,C}
, there exists
f_{2}\in{\mathcal{F}}_{m,P}
with
P=O(Nm(\log(\frac{m}{(\gamma^{\prime\prime})^{2}})+\log R^{\prime\prime}))

nonzero
number of parameters that
\frac{5}{6}
-robustly memorize
{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}
, where

\displaystyle\gamma^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle:=(1-\frac{5}{6})\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}}\overset{(a)}{\geq}\frac{1}{6}\times\frac{12}{5}\rho\epsilon_{\mathcal{D}}=\frac{2}{5}\rho\epsilon_{\mathcal{D}},

\displaystyle R^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle:=\max_{i\in[N]}\left\lVert f_{1}({\bm{x}}_{i})\right\rVert_{2}=\max_{i\in[N]}\left\lVert\sigma({\bm{W}}{\bm{x}}_{i}+{\bm{b}})\right\rVert_{2}=\max_{i\in[N]}\left\lVert{\bm{W}}{\bm{x}}_{i}+{\bm{b}}\right\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{i\in[N]}\left\lVert{\bm{W}}{\bm{x}}_{i}\right\rVert_{2}+\left\lVert{\bm{b}}\right\rVert_{2}\leq 3R.

Here (a) is by
Equation

75
.

Now, we claim that
f:=f_{2}\circ f_{1}

\rho
-robustly memorize
{\mathcal{D}}
.
For any
i\in[N]
, take
{\bm{x}}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{2}({\bm{x}}_{i},\rho\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}})
.
Then, by
Equation

74
, we have
f_{1}({\bm{x}})={\bm{W}}{\bm{x}}+{\bm{b}}
and
f_{1}({\bm{x}}_{i})={\bm{W}}{\bm{x}}_{i}+{\bm{b}}
so that

\displaystyle\left\lVert f_{1}({\bm{x}})-f_{1}({\bm{x}}_{i})\right\rVert_{2}=\left\lVert{\bm{W}}{\bm{x}}-{\bm{W}}{\bm{x}}_{i}\right\rVert_{2}\leq\left\lVert{\bm{x}}-{\bm{x}}_{i}\right\rVert_{2}\leq\rho\epsilon_{\mathcal{D}}.

(76)

Moreover, combining
Equations

75
and
76
results
\left\lVert f_{1}({\bm{x}})-f_{1}({\bm{x}}_{i})\right\rVert_{2}\leq\frac{5}{6}\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}}
.
Since
f_{2}

\frac{5}{6}
-robustly memorize
{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}
, we have

\displaystyle f({\bm{x}})=f_{2}(f_{1}({\bm{x}}))=f_{2}(f_{1}({\bm{x}}_{i}))=y_{i}.

In particular,
f({\bm{x}})=y_{i}
for any
{\bm{x}}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{2}({\bm{x}}_{i},\rho\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}})
, concluding that
f
is a
\rho
-robust memorizer
{\mathcal{D}}
.
Regarding the number of parameters to construct
f
, notice that
f_{1}
consists of
(d+1)m=\tilde{O}(d^{2}\rho^{2})
parameters (and thus
\tilde{O}(d^{2}\rho^{2})

nonzero
parameters) as
m=\tilde{O}(d\rho^{2})
.
f_{2}
consists of
\tilde{O}(Nm)=\tilde{O}(Nd\rho^{2})

nonzero
parameters.
Since the case IV assumes
N\geq d
, we have

\displaystyle d^{2}\rho^{2}\leq Nd\rho^{2}

Therefore,
f
in total consists of
\tilde{O}(d^{2}\rho^{2}+Nd\rho^{2})=\tilde{O}(Nd\rho^{2})
number of
nonzero
parameters.
This proves the theorem for the fourth case.

Case V:
\rho\in(1/5\sqrt{d},1/3)
, and
d>N\geq 600\log N
.

The last case combines the two techniques used in Cases III and IV.
We first apply
Proposition

B.21
to
{\mathcal{D}}
to obtain 1-Lipschitz linear
\varphi:{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{N}
such that
{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime}:=\{(\varphi({\bm{x}}_{i}),y_{i})\}_{i\in[N]}\in{\bm{D}}_{N,N,C}
has
\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime}}=\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}}
.
Note that we can apply the proposition since
d\geq N
.

Next, we apply
Proposition

B.19
to
{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime}\in{\bm{D}}_{N,N,C}
with
m=\max\{\lceil 9N\rho^{2}\rceil,\lceil 600\log N\rceil\}
and
\alpha=1/5
.
Let us first check that the specified
m
satisfies the condition
24\alpha^{-2}\log N\leq m\leq N
for the proposition to be applied.

\alpha=1/5
and
m\geq 600\log N
ensure the first inequality
24\alpha^{-2}\log N\leq m
.
The second inequality
m\leq N
is decomposed into two parts.
Since
\rho\leq\frac{1}{3}
, we have
9N\rho^{2}\leq N
so that

\displaystyle\lceil 9N\rho^{2}\rceil\leq N.

(77)

Moreover,
600\log N\leq N
implies

\displaystyle\lceil 600\log N\rceil\leq N.

(78)

Gathering
Equations

68
and
69
proves
m\leq N
.
Additionally, as
N\geq 2
, we have
N\geq 600\log N\geq 600\log 2\geq 400
.
By
Lemma

B.22
, this implies
10\log N\leq N
.

By the
Proposition

B.19
, there exists 1-Lipchitz linear mapping
\phi:{\mathbb{R}}^{N}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{m}
and
\beta>0
such that
{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}:=\{(\phi(\varphi({\bm{x}}_{i})),y_{i})\}_{i\in[N]}\in{\bm{D}}_{m,N,C}
satisfies

\displaystyle\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}}\geq\frac{4}{5}\beta\epsilon_{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime}.

(79)

As
m\geq 600\log N\geq 10\log N
, the inequality
\beta\geq\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\frac{m}{N}}
is also satisfied by
Proposition

B.19
.
Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\beta\geq\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\frac{m}{N}}\overset{(a)}{\geq}\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\frac{\lceil 9N\rho^{2}\rceil}{N}}\geq\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\frac{9N\rho^{2}}{N}}=\frac{3}{2}\rho,

(80)

where (a) is by the definition of
m
.
Moreover, since
\varphi
and
\phi
are both 1-Lipchitz linear,
\phi\circ\varphi:{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{m}
is also 1-Lipschitz linear.
Therefore,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\phi(\varphi({\bm{x}}_{i}))\right\rVert_{2}=\left\lVert\phi(\varphi({\bm{x}}_{i}-{\bm{0}}))\right\rVert_{2}=\left\lVert\phi(\varphi({\bm{x}}_{i}))-\phi(\varphi({\bm{0}}))\right\rVert_{2}\leq\left\lVert{\bm{x}}_{i}-{\bm{0}}\right\rVert_{2}=\left\lVert{\bm{x}}_{i}\right\rVert_{2},

(81)

for all
i\in[N]
.
Hence, by letting
R:=\max_{i\in[N]}\{\left\lVert{\bm{x}}_{i}\right\rVert_{2}\}
, we have
\left\lVert\phi(\varphi({\bm{x}}_{i}))\right\rVert_{2}\leq R
for all
i\in[N]
.

Now, we set the first layer hidden matrix as the matrix
{\bm{W}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{m\times d}
corresponding to
\phi\circ\varphi
under the standard basis of
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
and
{\mathbb{R}}^{m}
.
Moreover, set the first hidden layer bias as
{\bm{b}}:=2R{\bm{1}}=2R(1,1,\cdots,1)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{m}
.
Then, we have

\displaystyle{\bm{W}}{\bm{x}}+{\bm{b}}\geq{\bm{0}},

(82)

for all
{\bm{x}}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{2}({\bm{x}}_{i},\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}})
for all
i\in[N]
, where the comparison between two vectors are element-wise.
This is because for all
i\in[N],j\in[m]
and
{\bm{x}}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{2}({\bm{x}},\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}})
, we have

\displaystyle({\bm{W}}{\bm{x}}+{\bm{b}})_{j}=({\bm{W}}{\bm{x}})_{j}+2R\geq 2R-\left\lVert{\bm{W}}{\bm{x}}\right\rVert_{2}\overset{(a)}{\geq}2R-\left\lVert{\bm{x}}\right\rVert_{2}\overset{(b)}{\geq}2R-(R+\epsilon_{\mathcal{D}})\overset{(c)}{\geq}0,

where (a) is by
Equation

81
, (b) is by the triangle inequality, and (c) is due to
R\geq\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}}
.

We construct the first layer of the neural network as
f_{1}({\bm{x}}):=\sigma({\bm{W}}{\bm{x}}+{\bm{b}})
which includes the activation
\sigma
.
Next, we show that,
{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}:=\{(f_{1}({\bm{x}}_{i}),y_{i})\}_{i\in[N]}
satisfies

\displaystyle\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}}\geq\frac{6}{5}\rho\epsilon_{\mathcal{D}},

(83)

by the above properties.
This is because for
i\neq j
with
y_{i}\neq y_{j}
we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert f_{1}({\bm{x}}_{i})-f_{1}({\bm{x}}_{j})\right\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\sigma({\bm{W}}{\bm{x}}_{i}+{\bm{b}})-\sigma({\bm{W}}{\bm{x}}_{j}+{\bm{b}})\right\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{=}\left\lVert({\bm{W}}{\bm{x}}_{i}+{\bm{b}})-({\bm{W}}{\bm{x}}_{j}+{\bm{b}})\right\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\phi(\varphi({\bm{x}}_{i}))-\phi(\varphi({\bm{x}}_{j}))\right\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\geq}2\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}}

\displaystyle\overset{(c)}{\geq}2\times\frac{4}{5}\beta\epsilon_{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\overset{(d)}{\geq}2\times\frac{4}{5}\times\frac{3}{2}\rho\epsilon_{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\frac{12}{5}\rho\epsilon_{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\overset{(e)}{=}\frac{12}{5}\rho\epsilon_{\mathcal{D}},

where (a) is by
Equation

82
, (b) is by the definition of the
\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}}
, (c) is by
Equation

79
, (d) is by
Equation

80
, and (e) is because
\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime}}=\epsilon_{\mathcal{D}}
.

By
Lemma

E.3
applied to
{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}\in{\bm{D}}_{m,N,C}
, there exists
f_{2}\in{\mathcal{F}}_{m,P}
with
P=O(Nm(\log(\frac{m}{(\gamma^{\prime\prime})^{2}})+\log R^{\prime\prime}))

nonzero
number of parameters that
\frac{5}{6}
-robustly memorize
{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}
, where

\displaystyle\gamma^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle:=(1-\frac{5}{6})\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}}\overset{(a)}{\geq}\frac{1}{6}\times\frac{12}{5}\rho\epsilon_{\mathcal{D}}=\frac{2}{5}\rho\epsilon_{\mathcal{D}},

\displaystyle R^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle:=\max_{i\in[N]}\left\lVert f_{1}({\bm{x}}_{i})\right\rVert_{2}=\max_{i\in[N]}\left\lVert\sigma({\bm{W}}{\bm{x}}_{i}+{\bm{b}})\right\rVert_{2}=\max_{i\in[N]}\left\lVert{\bm{W}}{\bm{x}}_{i}+{\bm{b}}\right\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{i\in[N]}\left\lVert{\bm{W}}{\bm{x}}_{i}\right\rVert_{2}+\left\lVert{\bm{b}}\right\rVert_{2}\leq 3R.

Here, (a) is by
Equation

83
.

Now, we claim that
f:=f_{2}\circ f_{1}

\rho
-robustly memorize
{\mathcal{D}}
.
For any
i\in[N]
, take
{\bm{x}}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{2}({\bm{x}}_{i},\rho\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}})
.
Then, by
Equation

82
, we have
f_{1}({\bm{x}})={\bm{W}}{\bm{x}}+{\bm{b}}
and
f_{1}({\bm{x}}_{i})={\bm{W}}{\bm{x}}_{i}+{\bm{b}}
so that

\displaystyle\left\lVert f_{1}({\bm{x}})-f_{1}({\bm{x}}_{i})\right\rVert_{2}=\left\lVert{\bm{W}}{\bm{x}}-{\bm{W}}{\bm{x}}_{i}\right\rVert_{2}\leq\left\lVert{\bm{x}}-{\bm{x}}_{i}\right\rVert_{2}\leq\rho\epsilon_{\mathcal{D}}.

(84)

Moreover, putting
Equation

83
to
Equation

84
results
\left\lVert f_{1}({\bm{x}})-f_{1}({\bm{x}}_{i})\right\rVert_{2}\leq\frac{5}{6}\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}}
.
Since
f_{2}

\frac{5}{6}
-robustly memorize
{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime\prime}
, we have

\displaystyle f({\bm{x}})=f_{2}(f_{1}({\bm{x}}))=f_{2}(f_{1}({\bm{x}}_{i}))=y_{i}.

In particular,
f({\bm{x}})=y_{i}
for any
{\bm{x}}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{2}({\bm{x}}_{i},\rho\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}})
, concluding that
f
is a
\rho
-robust memorizer
{\mathcal{D}}
.

Regarding the number of
nonzero
parameters to construct
f
, notice that
f_{1}
consists of
(d+1)m=\tilde{O}(Nd\rho^{2})

nonzero
parameters as
m=\tilde{O}(N\rho^{2})
.
f_{2}
consists of
\tilde{O}(Nm)=\tilde{O}(N^{2}\rho^{2})

nonzero
parameters.
Since the case V assumes
N<d
, we have

\displaystyle N^{2}\rho^{2}

\displaystyle\leq Nd\rho^{2}.

Therefore,
f
in total consists of
\tilde{O}(Nd\rho^{2}+N^{2}\rho^{2})=\tilde{O}(Nd\rho^{2})
number of
nonzero
parameters.
This proves the theorem for the last case.

∎

Nonzero Parameter Counts: Existing Upper Bounds.

In
Section

1.1
, the existing upper bound is stated by counting all parameters.
When counting only the nonzero parameters, the corresponding existing upper bound takes a different form.
Specifically, for any dataset
{\mathcal{D}}
with input dimension
d
and size
N
, there exist a neural network that achieves robust memorization on
{\mathcal{D}}
with the robustness ratio
\rho
under
\ell_{2}
-norm, with the number of parameters
P
bounded as follows:

\displaystyle P=\begin{cases}\tilde{O}(N+d)&\text{if }\rho\in(0,1/{\sqrt{d}}].\\
\tilde{O}(Nd^{2}\rho^{4}+d)&\text{if }\rho\in(1/{\sqrt{d}},1/{\sqrt[4]{d}}].\\
\tilde{O}(Nd)&\text{if }\rho\in(1/{\sqrt[4]{d}},1).\\
\end{cases}\vskip-2.0pt

(85)

This is the counterpart to
Equation

2
that considers all parameter counts.
As in the case of full parameter count, the first and the third case in
Equation

85
directly follow from
Yu et al. 2024
and
Egosi et al. 2025
respectively.
The work by
Egosi et al. 2025
can be implicitly improved to the second case under the moderate
\rho
condition, using the same translation technique provided in
Section

D.3
.

E.4
Lemmas for Nonzero Parameter Count

Here, we state
Lemmas

D.1
and
D.2
—that corresponds to Theorem B.6 of
Yu et al. 2024
—to its original version that contains the
nonzero
parameter count with
\ell_{2}
-norm into the consideration.

Lemma E.3

(Theorem B.6,
Yu et al. 2024
)
.

For any
\mathcal{D}\in{\bm{D}}_{d,N,C}
and
\rho\in(0,1)
, define
\gamma:=(1-\rho)\epsilon_{{\mathcal{D}}}>0
and
R>1
with
\left\lVert{\bm{x}}_{i}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq R
for all
i\in[N]
.
Then, there exists a neural network
f
with width
O(d)
, depth
O(N(\log(\frac{d}{\gamma^{2}})+\log R))
that
\rho
-robustly memorizes
\mathcal{D}
using at most
O(Nd(\log(\frac{d}{\gamma^{2}})+\log R))

nonzero
parameters.
